# Release Notes

This file is the canonical release log for tracked Orchestrarium monorepo changes that matter at publication time.

## 2026-08-28

- **The final PR review fixes provider-script interpreter trust and makes release regressions portable across hosted checkout shapes.** Python-backed Codex and Claude bindings now canonicalize a symlinked host interpreter while rejecting a repository-controlled interpreter through the existing repository trust boundary; ProcessRunner remains the sole no-follow executable admission owner. Success/fatal-output fixtures supply exact synthetic credentials, zombie-aware settlement tests match the production census while retaining PID-reuse checks, the required `65ef…` stock-skill payload is a 20 KiB hash-bound fixture instead of an ambient Git-history dependency, and governance-cap tests bind the semantic owner rather than a physical line number. **Why it matters:** the same reviewed behavior now passes under virtual environments, unreaping containers, shallow/source-archive checkouts, and harmless documentation reflow without weakening production policy.
- **Process Runner now binds operating-system process creation to the exact executable object whose identity it reports.** Windows holds non-reparse leaf and ancestor handles that deny write, delete, and rename through suspended process creation; Linux executes the already-open descriptor through `/proc/self/fd` and retains a mandatory read lease for every owner-owned or effectively writable inode until child and process-group settlement. Partial acquisition is lifecycle-owned, cleanup uncertainty is visible, and pre-owner path checks are lexical only. **Why it matters:** a same-user pathname or in-place swap can no longer execute different code under the validated executable SHA-256, including Kimi and the shared provider, validator, and detached-validation consumers.
- **External terminal-ledger confirmation now streams canonical bounded JSONL instead of materializing the complete ledger.** The reader retains only one matching terminal candidate and duplicate count, bounds every physical line and nonblank record—including malformed and oversized records—against the compiled schema-checked limits, and still fails closed on malformed, duplicate, overflowed, truncated, or provenance-mismatched evidence. **Why it matters:** a near-limit ledger cannot exhaust wrapper memory or bypass the event budget while the provider result is being confirmed.
- **Non-opaque Codex and Claude launches now require explicit, nonempty environment credential scan coverage before prompt consumption, capture, ledger, or provider execution.** Codex cached authentication, the Claude subscription override, and every Claude Amazon Bedrock or Google Vertex AI credential mode—including token-only and complete-looking environment shapes—stop with `E_EXTERNAL_PROVIDER_CREDENTIAL_SCAN_UNAVAILABLE`; only direct Codex or Claude API-key/token modes retain exact raw-byte output scanning. Finalization independently rejects a launched non-opaque run whose authenticated configuration lacks that coverage, while Kimi remains its explicit opaque-session route with its existing machine-path and output safety scan. **Why it matters:** an empty scan-needle set can no longer be mistaken for proof that a refreshable file, keyring, or cloud credential cannot leak into external-provider output.
- **POSIX process supervision now treats conclusively dead zombie descendants as settled without hiding live process-group members.** The one `/proc` census owner excludes `Z` rows only when no running, sleeping, or stopped member remains, while provider and repository-transfer adapters still require complete pipe/resource settlement. Linux tests restore the caller's prior subreaper state on every path. **Why it matters:** a dead orphan cannot force a false settlement timeout, and test infrastructure no longer leaks process-global state.
- **The shipped-skill documentation now lists `$github-pr-review-bot` consistently across shared, Codex, and Claude surfaces.** README and Claude entrypoint projections match the common-skill owner, with parity and size guards updated. **Why it matters:** operators can discover the hosted PR-review workflow without documentation drift.
- **Windows Kimi enrollment now admits exact Kimi Code `0.39.1` while retaining `0.39.0` as the rollback identity.** The Process Runner and provider-prompt owners share one current-plus-rollback release registry, but every launch request also carries the exact enrolled pin identity and admission refuses a different accepted registry member. Ordinary enrollment remains create-only and refuses drift, while the explicit positive `--replace-kimi-enrollment` maintenance action validates the fixed new executable and atomically rotates only an accepted old pin under a race lock with rollback when the published pin and executable no longer match. Installer and wrapper maintenance stop before provider launch, and Kimi children retain `KIMI_CODE_NO_AUTO_UPDATE=1`. **Why it matters:** operators can upgrade the sealed Windows Kimi route without weakening executable attestation, silently adopting an unknown binary, or manually deleting the continuity pin; the known prior release remains a tested rollback path without becoming an unpinned launch substitute.
- **Claude transport projection upgrades now recognize the exact recent stock states at `448e8c5a`, `1a56f81f`, and `7192c914`.** Admission binds all eight transport members plus their manifest by SHA-256, then migrates only the changed provider transport members and manifest; any one-byte member drift still rejects the entire atomic state. **Why it matters:** a stock installation from any recent hotfix state can upgrade normally without weakening create-only ownership or admitting a customized projection.
- **Manual repository transfer now delegates every Git process tree to the shared Process Runner and makes output replacement explicitly opt-in.** The installed helper imports only the hash-bound canonical runner projection, uses a sealed repository-transfer Git request with a sanitized environment and 60-second production deadline, and returns only after Windows Job Object or POSIX process-group settlement. `inventory` and `bundle` refuse an existing output unless `--force` is supplied. An absent output uses one atomic no-replace link; a forced existing output is replaced through one `os.replace` only after its ordinary parent and exact ordinary file identity are rechecked immediately before publication. Links, reparse points, unsafe ancestors, type changes, and identity drift fail closed; a same-user substitution after that final check is explicitly outside the 1.x guarantee. `verify` and `cleanup` never accept the flag. **Why it matters:** a Git descendant cannot retain transfer pipes after the helper returns, and the normal path cannot overwrite an operator artifact or a raced output.
- **A separate `$github-pr-review-bot` common skill now owns the hosted Codex pull-request review loop for both Codex and Claude packs.** It binds every trigger and result to the current hosted head and exact comment, verifies reaction authors, distinguishes acknowledged `eyes`, clean `+1` or clean same-head reviews, and current finding threads, and forbids elapsed-time retriggers or CI reruns. Thread resolution occurs only after the exact fix reaches the remote head; bot PASS remains separate from human publication and merge gates. **Why it matters:** future sessions can operate the review bot consistently without duplicating triggers, burning CI quota, trusting stale local state, or hiding unresolved feedback inside Lead’s broader workflow.
- **Hook-health verification now runs through the existing Process Runner process-tree owner under one 60-second execution-and-settlement deadline.** Windows Job Object and POSIX process-group settlement reap descendants that inherit the health pipes; the final five seconds remain reserved for cleanup and rollback. Unlimited successful stdout stays disk-spooled only through a sealed, one-use capability bound to the exact canonical health script, complete request, sink, and ordinary spool identity, while strict failure stderr and interruption behavior remain unchanged. **Why it matters:** neither a silent child nor a surviving descendant can strand or outlive an install transaction, and the hook-only spool exception cannot be reused by another process profile.
- **Kimi’s canonical wrapper now owns the terminal response-format instruction required by its strict final-line oracle.** One closed verdict set derives both the rendered `GATE: PASS | REVISE | BLOCKED` grammar and the constant Kimi-only instruction appended after the unchanged caller bundle; payload preparation occurs before capture, launch-ledger, or provider side effects. Missing, duplicate, malformed, conflicting, or non-final gates still fail closed, and every Kimi result remains independently verified and nonauthorizing. **Why it matters:** callers no longer waste provider time or duplicate a private parser contract merely because their natural-language prompt omitted an undocumented final-line instruction.
- **External Codex and Claude launchers now bind executable provenance before reading authentication or prompt material.** Ambient-`PATH` discovery is rejected when the selected provider executable—or the PowerShell host used by the standalone Claude reserve wrapper—resolves inside the active repository; an explicit absolute provider binding and externally discovered executables remain supported. Kimi enrollment, Grok unavailability, Process Runner, provider profiles, and launch flags are unchanged. **Why it matters:** a repository-controlled executable cannot receive provider credentials merely by shadowing `codex`, `claude`, or `pwsh` on `PATH`.
- **Publication-safety range scans now resolve and continuously bind one explicitly modeled source revision instead of silently scanning the current checkout.** Ordinary `--range REMOTE DST` uses `DST` as its source; the Git-push gate may supply its already-frozen object through `--range-source`, and the version-3 receipt reports separate `dst`, `src`, and resolved `tip` identities. Missing, non-commit, or drifted sources fail closed, symbolic and full-object sources select the same commit, and unrelated `HEAD` movement cannot change the result. The gate independently requires receipt remote and destination to equal its frozen push target and both source and tip to equal its frozen source object, while retaining current-`HEAD`, bounded-execution, and process-reaping checks. **Why it matters:** a receipt cannot hide candidate B being scanned as branch A, closing both false-clean and false-finding publication outcomes without weakening destination correlation.
- **Global upgrades now recognize the exact previously installed PR-3 Lead, Claude transport, and manual-transfer states before replacing them with the current provider, publication, and Git-free verification fixes.** Cache-neutral Lead digests `a90c1989...`, `2600ed4c...`, `3ca341a8...`, `3067dcad...`, `52f2c3d2...`, `d6cf6e5c...`, `0da7db45...`, `1e4bdc38...`, and `1f870106...` are accepted only for `lead`, while `b73cde79...` and `e0d160e3...` are accepted only for `manual-repo-transfer`. The exact nine-member Claude transport tuples from `448e8c5a`, `1a56f81f`, and `7192c914` are each accepted only as one atomic predecessor, so this update replaces only their changed transport members and manifest. Existing transactional migration keeps already-current state byte-preserving, and one-byte, extra-file, or mixed-tuple drift still fails closed. **Why it matters:** machines already running any recognized PR-3 hotfix state can install this follow-up normally instead of requiring a manual overwrite, without weakening create-only ownership for customized skills or partial transport projections.
- **The repository publication gate now validates current active work without turning unrelated historical task-memory hygiene into a tracked-code blocker.** `check-publication-gate.py` invokes the existing work-item checker in explicit `--active-only` mode, which retains category, active-ledger, stale-run, dependency, epic, and strict open-`REVISE` checks for every current item while skipping only the periodic archived-ledger obligation sweep. The checker default remains the unchanged full active-plus-archive audit, so legacy archive defects stay visible and lifecycle close still prevents laundering at the owning transaction. **Why it matters:** a valid current pull request is no longer blocked by pre-existing local-only archives that require a separately designed migration, without weakening current-task or archive-creation validation.
- **External provider evidence now binds the exact resolved launch flags instead of collapsing runs to model and effort.** Provider-result Version 2 envelopes and both external launch/terminal ledger events carry one bounded, provider-specific `launchFlags` string array, so runs with different sandbox, input/output, permission, or tool modes remain distinguishable even when their model and effort match. Positional prompts, arbitrary configuration, path-bearing, credential-bearing, malformed, control-character, and oversized bindings fail closed; prompts still travel through the existing file or standard-input channel, `realization` remains unsupported, and historical Version 2 records without `launchFlags` remain readable. **Why it matters:** an execution artifact now preserves the exact admitted provider profile it claims without copying prompt text, credentials, machine paths, capture paths, or an invented provider-realization claim.
- **Receiver-side archive verification no longer requires Git when no repository is supplied.** `manual-repo-transfer verify --bundle <zip>` now reaches the archive-integrity verifier without `--git-executable`; payload-source and trusted source modes still reject a missing explicit Git executable at the existing mode-validation boundary before repository binding. **Why it matters:** a receiver can validate the transfer ZIP on a machine without Git while every repository-aware verification path preserves strict external Git binding.
- **Rootless baseline ledger validation no longer fails on absent legacy projection state.** The manifest-bound projection reader now distinguishes an ordinary caller with no projection inputs before applying strict active/archive target identity, returning the original events with zero projection counters and no projection error. Explicit manifest or registry candidates still require the unchanged fail-closed target identity, and real active, archived, transactional, and physical-drift projection checks remain strict. **Why it matters:** callers can validate ledger data outside a repository-owned `work-items` tree without a false migration-target error, while candidate projection data cannot bypass repository identity controls.
- **Installer tree materialization now excludes ordinary Python runtime-cache bytecode whenever cache-insensitive identity is selected.** Fresh create-only skill installation and hash-pinned accepted-prior replacement skip only regular `.pyc` files beneath `__pycache__`, pruning cache directories only when they become empty. Standalone `.pyc` files, non-cache content inside `__pycache__`, type and reparse-point checks, and create-only collision failures remain strict; an already-installed cache-only tree still returns byte-for-byte unchanged, while an admitted stock migration removes its old runtime cache. **Why it matters:** source-tree interpreter caches can no longer leak into a new or migrated installed skill merely because the installer correctly excluded those same transient bytes from canonical identity.
- **The pure Version 3 solution-attempt reducer now rejects malformed derived state before replay or transition dispatch.** One strict validator binds the current state to its decoded bootstrap capsule and verifies operation-head identity, immutable object and initial-attempt projections, active attempts, rejection and review buckets, reassessment frontiers, and launch/terminal pairing. Invalid nested records and dangling identifiers return `SOL-E001-STATE-INVALID` with the input state unchanged instead of committing corrupt projections or raising from a later dereference. **Why it matters:** installed Codex and Claude reducers now fail closed on corrupted serialized state while preserving deterministic, side-effect-free reduction and every valid generated transition.
- **Provider completion now counts fatal standard-error markers across the entire bounded capture.** Finalization no longer applies a second 64 KiB scan cap after Process Runner has already settled a complete, non-truncated capture, so late `ERROR`, `FATAL`, and `API Error` lines contribute to the exact published `stderrMarkerCount` and prevent a false clean verdict. **Why it matters:** long provider diagnostics cannot hide fatal lines that occur after the first 64 KiB; existing capture limits, early-marker behavior, Kimi output safety, nonzero-exit precedence, and raw-stderr redaction remain unchanged.
- **Manual repository transfer now binds the physical repository and Git executable before any child process and preserves non-UTF-8 path bytes without changing ordinary Unicode Version 1 manifests.** Aliases and subdirectories resolve to one strict physical `.git` root; the external reparse-free Git binary is identity/hash checked around launches and its reported top-level must match. Surrogateescape path bytes use conditional JSON escapes and remain hostile metadata-only entries requiring an exact external receipt, never payload, deletion, ancestor inclusion, or ZIP placement. **Why it matters:** aliases cannot hide a repository-contained executable or redirect the helper across repository boundaries, while POSIX raw-byte filenames remain inventoryable. Schema version 1, preview-only cleanup, and ordinary Unicode bytes are preserved; a same-user replacement after the final pre-open check remains outside the guarantee.

- **The Windows Codex push gate now survives host shell-label and working-directory drift without weakening publication checks.** When Codex reports `Bash` for an exact PowerShell literal, preflight may recover the host dialect only if exactly one supported parser yields one byte-exact, canonical, direct publication command; simple, normalized, ambiguous, wrapped, or near-match forms retain the existing fail-closed result. Preflight binds the current call's explicit absolute `workdir` (or the authoritative envelope `cwd` only when that field is absent) lexically; only after strict pull-request shape or generic grammar admission does the heavy gate resolve it, prove it is exactly the Git top-level, bind Git and GitHub executables outside that selected root, and pass the same exact Git executable and root to every repository-local oracle and scanner child without changing process-global cwd. When the host drops a requested tool workdir, the PR-scoped gate also accepts exactly one six-argument absolute literal, `git -C <absolute-root> push <remote> HEAD:refs/heads/<head>`; explicit tool workdir must normalize to the same root, while relative, missing, duplicate, conflicting, or near-match `-C` shapes deny before the oracle, and generic routing is unchanged. The installed shared `.agents/skills/lead` path is now an explicit Codex gate identity, and pull-request oracle children receive the remaining 45-second aggregate budget instead of an unrelated 8-second child cap. **Why it matters:** a valid reviewed pull-request push no longer fails as `PRG-COMMAND-SHAPE`, false remote mismatch, or false branch drift solely because of Codex tool metadata or a public `git ls-remote` taking longer than eight seconds; relative, missing, non-repository, conflicting nested workdirs, and selected-root executable substitution fail closed, while absolute Git identity, one-command grammar, pull-request oracle, authoritative range scan, and human publication approval remain unchanged.

- **The installed Kimi wrapper can now enroll and verify its fixed Windows executable binding directly.** `--enroll-executable` atomically creates the same path/size/SHA-256 pin used by the global installer, exact replay is byte-preserving, and any different existing pin or release identity still fails closed. Read-only `--verify-enrollment` checks the current binary content without launching Kimi, while missing or malformed binding errors print the exact installed wrapper enrollment command. The wrapper does not inspect or manage Kimi authentication and never trusts `PATH` or `KIMI_BIN`. **Why it matters:** an operator can restore a missing local binding immediately without a full pack reinstall or a second binding implementation, while malformed or drifted state still requires explicit disposition.

## 2026-08-27

- The git-push publication gate now preserves its fixed transcript bounds while recovering the final pull-request authorization state from a stable, complete-record transcript suffix when full history exceeds the cap. Existing exact URL grants continue through the unchanged pull-request oracle and fresh version-3 publication scan, while revocations, malformed reserved markers, split records, transcript mutation, invalid data, and suffixes without authorization still deny publication.

- Kimi terminal capture now freezes one complete ten-field execution-provenance value at policy admission and reuses it unchanged for both the provider envelope and durable ledger. The ledger records the truthful native-effort state `unsupported`, keeps it outside ordered effort tiers, and read-back verifies every persisted provenance field before accepting the terminal row.

- Installed skill-pack validation now recognizes the current split global layout: Codex governance and contracts remain under `.codex`, while Orchestrarium skills and Lead runtime scripts resolve from `.agents/skills`. A stale legacy `.codex/skills` directory or junction no longer overrides that current layout; genuine legacy-only and project-local layouts remain supported.

- Global upgrades now migrate nine exact hash-pinned previously shipped stock skill trees through the existing rollback-safe accepted-prior path: Consultant, External Brigade, External Reviewer, External Worker, Graphics Engineer, Init Project, Lead, Second Opinion, and Visualization Engineer. Each prior is admitted only for its matching skill name; the Lead prior additionally pins the fully proven 54-file stock baseline plus its committed publication-gate hotfix. Customized trees, one-byte drift, and the same bytes under another skill name remain fail-closed. This lets operators upgrade legitimate older global installs without manually overwriting canonical skills or weakening create-only ownership.

- **Documentation now reflects the current Kimi and Luna contracts, superseding older disabled-state summaries without rewriting historical entries.** Kimi is explicit-only, nonauthorizing read-only exploration, research, planning, or review through the canonical fixed `kimi-code/k3` no-tools/no-subagents wrapper and never enters `auto`; Grok remains unavailable. Luna now requires an enabled feature and valid exact caller plan before the native-required path, otherwise returns `E_NATIVE_V2_DISABLED`; it remains zero-decision, exact-tool/root/oracle bound, and never falls back externally or to another model. Installer documentation now identifies hash-pinned recognized prior working or currently-disabled stock role payloads as the upgrade path and keeps customized payloads fail-closed. **Why it matters:** operators can distinguish an explicitly admitted bounded route from an unavailable one and preserve the native corridor's containment and provenance requirements during upgrades.

- **Global Codex upgrades now recognize six exact previously shipped `7872d36d` skill trees.** Consultant, Design Panel, Init Project, Manual Repo Transfer, Review Loop, and Second Opinion are admitted only as complete hash-pinned stock trees through the existing rollback-safe accepted-prior migration. Python interpreter caches (`__pycache__` with `.pyc` files) are excluded from canonical identity for every skill tree, matching the existing Lead behavior; current trees remain no-ops, while source, documentation, configuration, and arbitrary non-cache additions still fail closed. **Why it matters:** an operator can upgrade that legitimate prior install to the current explicit Kimi policy without manually overwriting canonical skills or weakening create-only ownership.

## 2026-08-26

- **Windows Kimi 0.38.0 is now admitted through one sealed read-only bundle profile.** The sole provider transport fixes model `kimi-code/k3`, text output, a constant tiny prompt, an explicit no-tools/no-subagents agent bundle, and an empty skills directory inside a fresh OS-temp capture root; `ProcessRunnerV1` rechecks executable version/content and sealed bundle identity/digest immediately before process creation, while generic native Windows command-line execution stays denied. The explicit global Codex `--enroll-kimi` operation pins only the fixed user Kimi path by normalized path, size, and SHA-256; runtime rejects `KIMI_BIN`, `PATH`, malformed pins, reparse paths, and pin drift. Kimi remains explicit-only, independently verified, nonauthorizing, and outside production `auto`; Grok remains unavailable in 1.x. **Why it matters:** operators can use Kimi for bounded broad research/review without live-repository tool access, an unobserved argv shape, second process owner, quota canary, or hidden native-effort claim.

- **Bounded process supervision is now active for the three approved 1.x consumer families.** The standard-library-only `process_supervision/process_runner.py` now owns provider-prompt operations, skill-pack validator child checks, and detached Slice A validation; other subprocess paths remain unchanged. Each migrated path receives exact argument-vector launch, complete standard-input delivery, bounded prefix-and-tail output capture, deadline and cancellation handling, and full descendant-process-tree settlement through the existing Windows Job Object or cooperative POSIX owner. On Windows, only Python and Git child shapes are admitted, through runner-owned executable-specific probes performed for the same run; adapters cannot self-attest. Native Codex and Claude fail early with a typed unavailable result on Windows, while Linux Codex and Claude launches are active; no macOS/Darwin backend is shipped; Kimi and Grok remain unavailable in 1.x. The projection manifest binds the final provider and runner bytes, while the installer admits only the exact published `8f92dc73`, `d1309ee5`, and `f87414e7` transport sets for rollback-safe atomic upgrade; customized files still fail closed and transaction failure restores original bytes and identities. **Why it matters:** a child can no longer recreate the 65 GB incident by feeding unbounded output into these consumers, and a direct process exit can no longer be mistaken for completion while descendants remain unsettled. The generic Windows command-line interface remains fail-closed unavailable; a Rust implementation remains deferred to 2.0, and active authorized same-user path or executable substitution plus a general launcher sandbox remain outside the 1.x guarantee.

- **Specialist handoffs now require an explicit Cleanup disposition.** Returned artifacts classify owned child process trees, temporary or capture paths, and isolation worktrees as `cleaned`, `preserved` with a reason, or `none`; provider-pack validators enforce the contract. **Why it matters:** delivery evidence can no longer omit the fate of resources created by the assigned lane.

- **Push publication safety now scans the complete unpublished Git object history and authorizes only Version 3 receipts.** The canonical range scanner covers every unpublished commit, tree, blob, and commit/path/blob subject, reads each unique blob once in bounded chunks, preserves path-local detector exemptions, records explicit text/binary disposition, and fails closed on incomplete coverage, limits, timeouts, cancellation, tip drift, or cleanup failure. The generic and pull-request gate routes now reject Version 2 and mixed-version evidence. **Why it matters:** secrets cannot be hidden by sanitizing, renaming, merging, or deleting a file before the final tip. Existing approval, route grammar, source-closure capture, one-use binding, tracked/path diagnostics, and human publication review remain unchanged.

- **Codex native-role installation now rejects a top-level inline `agents = { ... }` table before transaction planning.** The representation-aware preflight ignores comments and string content, preserves ordinary `[agents]` tables and dotted `agents.*` keys, and reparses every proposed config payload before admitting the plan. **Why it matters:** an install or dry-run can no longer report success after appending role tables into syntactically invalid TOML, while supported operator config bytes and create-only collision behavior remain unchanged.

- **Automated Claude dispatch now refuses unscannable helpers, resolves explicit authentication independently of home settings, and enforces taxonomy-owned role provenance before side effects.** Orchestrarium 1.x rejects every user-settings `apiKeyHelper` as `E_EXTERNAL_PROVIDER_API_KEY_HELPER_UNSUPPORTED` before prompt capture or provider lookup instead of launching with no credential scan needle. Explicit Anthropic key/token, Amazon Bedrock, Google Vertex AI, and subscription-override modes are selected and conflict-checked before settings lookup, so a minimal POSIX service does not need `HOME`; `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` is validated only when actually inspected or forwarded. Every explicit role whose byte-attested taxonomy disposition is `none` now fails as `E_EXTERNAL_PROVENANCE_ROLE_UNSUPPORTED`, including the current `$knowledge-archivist` disposition, while an omitted role alone remains `none/none`. The seven-member installed transport upgrades atomically from the exact `8f92dc73` prior. **Why it matters:** helper-returned credentials cannot leak through result materialization, valid explicit cloud or environment credentials are not blocked by unrelated filesystem state, and unsupported owner/adapter assignments cannot reach authentication, capture, ledger, binary probing, or provider execution.

- **Solution-attempt runtime owners now live in typed module directories.** The pure reducer moved to `scripts/solution_attempt/reducer.py`, the durable operation store moved to `scripts/agent_run_persistence/operation_store.py`, and the route activation registry moved beside its process supervisor at `scripts/process_supervision/route_activation_registry.py`. Production Codex and Claude installs now include only the pure reducer; the persistence and activation owners remain source-only. **Why it matters:** module placement now reflects ownership and lifecycle without compatibility shims, while public symbols, Version 3 behavior, and the persisted `agent_run_store.commit_operation` owner identity remain unchanged.

## 2026-08-25

- **Global Claude upgrades now preserve linked projection authority and settle migrations transactionally.** The installer verifies the linked Claude subroots before and after projection work, validates the helper from attested bytes rather than reopening its pathname, restores the prior tree if promotion or cleanup fails, and ignores only ordinary Python runtime-cache bytecode when comparing the canonical Lead tree. **Why it matters:** operators can safely upgrade a linked global Claude runtime without accepting a retargeted authority, cache-only false collision, or stranded projection residue after a failed upgrade.

- **Kimi and Grok remain visible as explicit policy routes, but are unavailable and disabled in 1.x.** The resolver may describe a requested route, yet it authorizes neither a launcher nor a provider probe. **Why it matters:** operators cannot mistake a configured name for a usable provider or accidentally turn an advisory route into an unreviewed external execution path; production `auto` routing remains Codex/Claude only.

- **The external-prompt governance capsule now has one canonical source and one installed projection.** Author it at `shared/external-prompt-governance.md`; installed wrappers receive `scripts/external-prompt-governance.md` and prefix that projection in memory. **Why it matters:** provider wrappers share one current boundary without copying stale prompt policy into role documents.

- **Repository transfer preparation is now available as the knowledge-style `$manual-repo-transfer` skill.** It inventories dirty, ignored, recovery, and runtime state, then creates and validates a selected transfer bundle while leaving cleanup preview-only. **Why it matters:** moving machines can preserve local evidence without treating a clean Git status as proof that nothing else matters. Actual transfer waits for current tasks and pull-request gates to pass.

- **Luna native execution is fail-closed in 1.x.** `mechanical-scout` now returns `E_LUNA_EXECUTION_CONTAINMENT_UNAVAILABLE` until host-enforced execution containment and attestation exist; `mechanical-worker` remains distinct with `E_LUNA_WRITE_CONTAINMENT_UNAVAILABLE`. `LunaExecutionContractV1` and `ScoutFactsV1` validation remain future prerequisites, not spawn authority. **Why it matters:** a rejected runtime falsifier cannot be retried into a shared-workspace execution, and interpretive work stays with a non-Luna owner.

- **Global installation now documents platform-specific home resolution.** POSIX accepts `HOME` (or `USERPROFILE` when present), while Windows requires `USERPROFILE`. **Why it matters:** an otherwise valid global installation does not silently target a different home directory on Windows.

## 2026-08-23

- **Slice B adds one shared, root-authored external prompt transport for explicit Kimi and official Grok routes.** `provider_prompt.py` and the two thin provider entries are projected byte-identically into the canonical and Claude-host lead trees and are rejected on projection drift. Kimi is pinned to K3, runs against a neutral scratch snapshot, and reports that native effort control is unavailable instead of silently choosing one. Grok is explicit-only behind its signed-binary, model/auth/capability/result-shape, and live-root read-only immutability gates. **Why it matters:** broad-context Kimi analysis and official Grok review can now be used safely in the bounded 1.x lanes without changing existing Codex/Claude `auto` routing. **Preserved:** only eligible read-only exploration, planning, and review pass the pure pre-dispatch policy; each result requires independent verification and cannot authorize a critical decision, commit, push, release, or publication; NPM, a generic provider registry, and provider-profile removal remain out of scope.

- **Codex global hook installation now supports an operator-selected `hooks.json` file symlink whose referent path may also cross directory symlinks or Windows junctions, without weakening inventory authority.** The installer preserves every link/junction object, resolves one bounded component chain to the final ordinary file, writes `codex-hook-inventory.json` beside that real target through the existing transaction, and binds each supported component's identity/kind/raw target plus the resolved target/parent/inventory identities so later replacement or retargeting fails closed. Dangling components, directory leaf targets, cycles, opaque reparse points, and alternate or linked inventories remain invalid; ordinary-file and file-link-only installs retain their existing layout and bytes. **Why it matters:** operators may keep `$HOME/.codex/hooks.json` pointed through shared junction-backed storage such as a synchronized environment volume without the install rolling back at hook-health verification, while registration rollback and report/require trust gates retain one authoritative path.
- **All 17 manifest native roles are now registered as callable Codex roles instead of merely copying TOML files.** Project and global installs derive each `[agents.<name>]` description and `config_file = "agents/<relativePath>"` from the already-validated source role, append only missing blocks, preserve every unrelated config byte/comment/key and `agents.max_concurrent_threads_per_session`, and fail without mutation on any same-name mapping collision. An absent config receives `multi_agent_v2 = true` plus all mappings. The exact frozen `agents.luna_mechanical` block and hash-pinned `agents/luna-mechanical.toml` migrate transactionally to the manifest set; a missing legacy file is allowed, while differing mapping/file state is preserved and blocks the install. **Why it matters:** Codex no longer emits `config_file must point to an existing file` for the retired legacy entry, and every installed specialist TOML is actually addressable by native dispatch without weakening create-only role ownership or reserializing operator config.

## 2026-08-22

- Codex now ships two narrowly scoped native Luna roles: read-only `mechanical-scout` and bounded-write `mechanical-worker`. They use exact `gpt-5.6-luna` at high reasoning effort only for deterministic mechanical tasks, are installed through the existing create-only protection, and are native-only with no external, Terra, or Sol fallback. This gives operators a least-privilege mechanical lane without changing agents-mode, existing Terra/Sol specialist roles, or automatic provider routing.
- Slice A confines every Luna-backed profile to those two roles and resolves each handoff through policy-only `RoleDispatchPolicyV1`: denied, native-disabled unavailable, or native-required. Host outcomes remain nonauthorizing caller observations and cannot be replayed. The detached supervisor runs one candidate focused-validation worktree and emits only bounded nonauthorizing summaries after child and cleanup settlement; independent QA and the existing human publication gate retain final authority.
- **Installed Native Luna dispatch now uses the same validated policy as the source monorepo.** The provider-neutral canonical `lead` payload stages the resolver plus its policy and non-owning manifest, while project/global callers select only exact resolver and `.codex/agents` locations. Foreign working directories and decoys are ignored; missing, drifted, reparse, or ambiguous inputs fail closed, and installed generic agents-mode resolution remains unsupported. **Why it matters:** installed Lead callers no longer reference a resolver that was absent from the payload, without adding fallback discovery or turning the manifest into ownership evidence.
- **Codex and Claude installs now share one deterministic canonical `lead` payload and fail with exact, recoverable diagnostics.** An explicit empty runtime-copy plan stays empty, both provider orders succeed against the same project or global-equivalent root, and existing Codex configuration is accepted byte-exact only for valid TOML feature shapes; malformed or wrongly typed states remain untouched and fail closed. Codex hook inventory is authoritative only as the exact ordinary sibling of the selected `hooks.json`. Hook-health failures cross the child boundary through a strict 4,096-byte typed envelope, while successful output is spooled and replayed byte-for-byte without a size policy. Rollback now continues independent created-object and snapshot settlement after an identity refusal, preserves the original cause, and reports retained recovery backups only when restoration cannot complete. **Why it matters:** reinstall order, malformed operator config, alternate inventory paths, large health output, and one failed cleanup can no longer produce false success, destroy the useful cause, or suppress disjoint restoration. **Preserved:** create-only user-byte polarity, installed receipt exclusion, existing provider routing and `auto` order, Node Package Manager scope, and the deferred 2.0 update/reclaim migration remain unchanged.

Update `RELEASE_NOTES.md` in the same change whenever tracked modifications affect installed behavior, governance, routing, role contracts, install surface, developer or operator workflow, or other release-relevant user-facing expectations.

Release-note entries should explain improvements, not just name them. For each release-relevant entry, state what changed, why it matters, which user or operator workflow is affected, and any important preserved behavior, boundary, or exemption when that context helps the reader understand the impact.

Keep the log in reverse-chronological `## YYYY-MM-DD` sections. Add new explanatory bullets under the current date heading, or create today's date heading if it is missing. Do not keep a long-lived `## Unreleased` bucket or silently rewrite older dated sections in place.

Do not add entries for purely local-only hygiene edits such as formatting, link fixes, report-only churn, scratch cleanup, archive moves, or non-semantic wording cleanup.

## 2026-08-18

- Current decision reads now accept only the frozen, manifest-admitted legacy YAML-frontmatter V0 records alongside the canonical leading-list V1 format. A strict repository-local manifest binds every admitted legacy path to its SHA-256 bytes and accepted policy decision before status reads, so copied, backdated, missing, changed, stale, or reappearing legacy records fail closed; decision bodies stay opaque and all authoring remains V1-only. The lifecycle owner remains generic, retirement mutation is explicitly outside this read-compatibility slice, and existing archives and unrelated lifecycle behavior are unchanged.

- Current decision audits now recognize a separate read-only H1 profile for exactly 13 frozen leading-list records admitted by a repository-local path-and-SHA-256 manifest. This lets repository operators audit those historical decisions truthfully while any new, copied, backdated, missing, changed, stale, or retired-then-reappearing record fails closed before lifecycle evaluation. The canonical V1 format remains the sole write format, the existing 53-record YAML-frontmatter V0 profile and grammar are unchanged, no H1 retirement writer is introduced, and PR598/PR600 recovery and registry-data rewrites remain outside this compatibility slice.

- The warn-only stray-artifact guard now covers four command-derived mistakes: unrequested `git worktree add`, mangled Windows redirect targets, build/log redirects into a repository root, and compiler invocations that would emit artifacts into that root. Root-sensitive checks require a confirmed repository-root working directory and stay silent after an explicit directory change; directed compiler output, scratch-subdirectory output, non-artifact redirects, and well-formed external Windows paths remain allowed. Warnings continue through the model-visible `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext` channel with exit code 0 and never block. This closes the operator-documentation gap for the behavior introduced by `950a799` without changing its production logic.

The original bytes remain auditable, valid closures and malformed events remain untargetable, reopened obligations still require an ordinary authorized replacement, V3 state stays independent, and the dedicated writer reports success only after atomic replacement plus exact readback. The sole operational procedure lives in the execution-tracking runbook; installation and overview documents remain pointers.

The contract replaces coordinate-freeze and API-ban heuristics with a causal, eight-dimension rule; it preserves identity and task state on equivalent refreshes while explicitly permitting bounded accessibility, localization, responsive, structural, lifecycle, corrective, and user-intended changes. Live Codex and Claude roles will consume one English-only neutral installed contract leaf through the existing installer workflow, without installing the maintainer reference tree or creating provider-specific semantic copies.

## 2026-08-12

- Common-skill validation now treats Codex and Claude Code as the live parity pair while independently pinning the deprecated Gemini and Qwen examples as frozen per-pack snapshots. The generic runtime policy map was removed; standalone production validators retain their provider-local pins, and this change introduces no npm dependency or frozen-source edit.

- Architecture-pattern guidance is now applicability-first: Lead routes only evidence-shaped cases, Architect records `selected | rejected | deferred` dispositions, and Architecture Reviewer independently verifies both positive and negative selection. This prevents cargo-cult pattern adoption while keeping zero selected patterns valid. Static source/install parity does not prove provider/model obedience; runtime fidelity remains `ASSUMPTION (UNVERIFIED)` until a separately pinned fresh-context report exists.

## 2026-08-11

- Claude and Codex design-panel bindings now use one fail-closed candidate-isolation rule: native Agent/subagent lanes are ineligible while sealing is unverified, and candidates run only through the existing direct external-worker boundary. External failure remains visible and may not fall back to an internal candidate.

- The git-push publication gate now registers a minimal fixed-sibling import runner for `hook_common.py` and the unchanged policy module. Warm launches can use Python's standard bytecode cache; load or delegation failures deny without raw detail. The changed Codex command identity requires interactive trust review after the next install.

- Lead correction retries and autonomous review-loop rounds now have separate owners: the shared spine alone owns the generic consecutive same-role/same-artifact `REVISE` limit, while `review_loop_state.py` exposes the explicit `REVIEW_LOOP_ROUND_CAP` for the independently bounded multi-angle loop. Existing numeric limits and explicit `--cap` overrides are unchanged.

- The passive-polling Stop guard now resolves the dispatched-review environment policy once at its process entrypoint and injects an immutable typed configuration into decision logic. The existing non-empty-value suppression behavior is unchanged, while lower hook logic no longer reads ambient process policy.

### Fixed

- **Retired SEN-2 delivery credit no longer leaves a live `Delivery action` schema in the periodic work-item checker.** The checker now requires only the current epic-location capability from the sentinel owner; the unused delivery parser, validator, failure ID, skipped behavior suite, and generated-copy residue are removed together. Existing `## Delivery action` headings are ordinary inert Markdown rather than an undocumented validation contract. **Why it matters:** work-item validation no longer rejects or coordinates against a field that no shipped registry entry consumes. **Preserved:** SEN-0, SEN-1, epic and lifecycle validation, fail-closed sentinel dependency loading, canonical/Claude/Codex mirror parity, historical release chronology, hook registration, installers, and publication gates are unchanged.

- **Codex skill-catalog validation now measures the model-visible runtime catalog instead of failing on a static estimate that cannot see runtime shortening.** The validator still reports the portable ownership estimate, but its default installed-environment verdict now comes from the provider-free `codex debug prompt-input` surface and distinguishes complete descriptions, runtime shortening, missing pack identities, malformed evidence, and unavailable execution. **Why it matters:** the current installed catalog retains all 45 Orchestrarium skill identities while Codex shortens eight descriptions; this is now a warning rather than a false omission failure, while a real missing identity or unusable runtime oracle still fails closed. **Preserved:** custom synthetic thresholds keep the deterministic static overflow gate, no provider prompt or network request is launched, and no skill, plugin, installer, Claude surface, or global installation is modified by validation.

- **Delivered work-items can no longer remain active while their related bugs drift independently.** The existing lifecycle owner now requires an exact `bug-dispositions.json` before close, covering every current bug whose parsed `context` equals the work-item slug. Each row explicitly terminalizes the bug or preserves it as a current residual with evidence; the owner applies those rows, archives the item, emits a bound receipt, and refreshes the generated README as one rollback-safe, replay-verifiable operation. The state checker rejects an unapplied active manifest. **Why it matters:** a completed and published task can no longer be forgotten in `active/`, nor can its bug registry be silently left stale. **Preserved:** unrelated bugs remain byte-identical, historical archives without the new manifest remain readable and replay-compatible, semantic disposition stays a Lead decision, `agent-runs.jsonl` review closure is not overloaded, and install/push remain separate explicit actions.

- **Windows publication-safety instructions now invoke the Python entrypoint that the pack actually ships.** Codex and Claude operator guidance plus the runtime-layout reference no longer point at a retired PowerShell wrapper that the production installer excludes and reclaims. Git Bash, macOS, and Linux continue to use the sibling shell wrapper; Windows PowerShell now runs `python .../check-publication-safety.py`. **Why it matters:** a clean install no longer directs Windows operators to a missing file. **Preserved:** scanner and push-gate logic, receipt semantics, installer cleanup, human review, explicit approval, and publication boundaries are unchanged.

## 2026-08-10

### Fixed

- **Push authorization now trusts only a fresh gate-owned canonical scanner snapshot, and scanner message coverage is bounded and exact.** The push gate derives its sibling Python scanner from the executing gate, opens and verifies that regular file, executes the captured bytes directly with the current trusted interpreter, and consumes only the bounded, reaped child result for the frozen remote, destination, source, `HEAD`, and tip. Manual or transcript-visible scanner output is diagnostic only. The scanner now gives every Git-object request a five-second deadline, reaps its reader on every return path, proves multiplicity-one equality across selected/requested/acquired/detector-completed commit object identifiers, and derives the version-2 receipt only from completed coverage. Findings, refusals, provenance failures, timeouts, overflow, cleanup failure, and identity drift remain redacted fail-closed denials. **Why it matters:** a same-basename lookalike or blocked reader can no longer mint clean push credit. **Preserved:** explicit user approval, solitary `--dry-run`, item-5 shell grammar, pull-request oracles, installers, launchers, staged/path manual checks, and the item-7 PowerShell-path follow-up are unchanged.

- **Publication scanning now covers the commit messages actually being pushed and no longer misses uppercase identifier boundaries.** The canonical scanner acquires the exact unpublished commit set once, scans each raw commit message plus current-tip blobs, recognizes separator, lower-camel, title-case, acronym, and all-caps identifier forms without treating glued lowercase text as an identifier, and emits one canonical message-complete `receipt=v2`. The Git-push gate parses that receipt once for both generic and strict pull-request routes and binds it to remote, destination, source, current `HEAD`, tip, order, and one-use state; tracked/path, legacy/v1, zero-commit, malformed, ambiguous, drifting, or mixed-version evidence denies. **Why it matters:** secrets in commit metadata and uppercase environment-style names can no longer receive false clean credit. **Operator workflow:** keep the default staged scan before commit; after the final commit, retry the same admissible push after correcting any named prerequisite so the gate owns a fresh authoritative check. Human review and explicit user publication approval are preserved; repository identity, remote URL/server freshness, and unrelated Git metadata remain outside the receipt.

- **Reusable Codex and Claude role packs no longer export Orchestrarium's project-only upgrade ledger into every installed repository.** The Lead and knowledge-archivist contracts no longer require or reconcile `work-items/roadmaps/orchestrator-upgrades.md`, and both provider validators now reject that exact path or an equivalent Orchestrator/Orchestrarium-upgrades obligation anywhere in installable skill or agent Markdown. **Why it matters:** installing or invoking the pack in an unrelated project can no longer turn absence of Orchestrarium's private promotion ledger into a local conformance gap. **Preserved:** generic project-relative work-item lifecycle, lessons, decisions, roadmap ownership, status-board reconciliation, and installer copy/idempotency behavior are unchanged; already-created foreign-project files are not modified by this source correction.

- **The publication gate now keeps solitary dry-run credit and its minimal seven-row registry while truthfully classifying the in-process test harness as cooperative, non-authoritative evidence.** The complete effective-publication graph grants the no-transcript fast path only to exactly one eligible direct positive long `--dry-run`; compound, mixed, candidate, child, nested, normalized, or malformed siblings continue through ordinary authorization. Every retained registry field remains consumed by the resolver/state machine. A clean cooperative row reports `contract-observed`. Arbitrary same-process mutation defeats harness observation and may run arbitrary caller code; the only proven invariant is that unchanged shipped source contains no external adapter or launcher, and its cooperative result has zero production/publication consumers. Exact factory/row types, one-use binding, actual six-phase deadline/cancellation checks, and canonical bounded capture remain deterministic test-correctness checks. Clean close purges every owner and resource record; cleanup failure retains one bounded retry record and successful retry returns to baseline. Each of the six external owners calls the literal unavailable factory once and stops at `ORACLE-AUTHORITY-UNAVAILABLE`, adapter/external `0/0`; no external adapter, launcher, discovery, target shell, real Git, network, provider, or fallback is shipped in the harness. **Why it matters:** the production safety boundary no longer rests on an impossible same-process anti-monkeypatch claim, hostile preparation input cannot execute caller protocols before typed composition, and repeated test sessions do not accumulate hidden lifecycle state. **Preserved:** F1-F4 parsing, the one exact positive solitary dry-run, genuine-user provenance, strict pull-request ordering, scanner/range receipts, transcript identity, canonical/mirror parity, hook-common, installers, and deprecated Gemini/Qwen surfaces.

## 2026-08-09

### Fixed

- **The Git-push publication gate now has one lossless command-analysis owner for shell confidence, wrapper execution, transcript dialect, and authorization projections.** Data masking is transactional: only complete dialect-valid POSIX heredocs, PowerShell here-strings, and block comments are excluded, while unsupported or unterminated syntax retains conservative literal push candidates. In an unquoted open PowerShell token, backtick plus line feed remains token content, while backtick plus carriage-return/line-feed is retained as an executable command boundary on Windows PowerShell 5.1 and PowerShell 7, so a following push cannot disappear. One declarative wrapper registry and one argument-vector state machine classify exact `eval`, `env`, `command`, `exec`, `sudo`, POSIX-shell, and PowerShell-host children; direct child argument vectors retain their original typed tokens without join/reparse, and unsupported publication-capable siblings become candidates. Each live or transcript occurrence preserves its actual tool name, resolves dialect once, and builds one immutable effective-publication projection consumed by push detection, scanner detection, receipt reuse, generic grammar, and strict pull-request shape. **Why it matters:** a literal multi-argument `eval` or nested direct-wrapper chain can no longer hide a publication, a selector after a script/file operand no longer fabricates a child, missing transcript dialect cannot mint scan credit, and every later direct, candidate, child, or nested publication consumes a receipt. `--dry-run` used as `-o`/`--exec`/`--repo` data still cannot open the fast path. **Preserved:** the genuine one-turn user override, an unambiguous standalone positive long-form dry-run, ordinary direct safe-option scan credit, scanner receipt format, strict pull-request canonical success/oracle/one-use receipt order, hook registration, canonical-to-mirror sync, and deprecated Gemini/Qwen surfaces. Opaque wrapper-file contents, runtime-generated expansion absent from typed literal input, runtime aliases/functions, generic repository identity, and generic source/tip binding remain explicit residuals. The test-only harness now has no external execution route: all six external-equivalence rows stop at the literal unavailable result with adapter/external `0/0`, while the in-process path is explicitly cooperative observation rather than target-host verification.

- **New scratch evidence now has a bounded, producer-declared terminal lifecycle without touching historical scratch.** The SessionStart watchdog resolves exactly one containing or direct-child Git repository within its existing shared 0.3-second budget. Terminal `PASS` ledger events may own bounded roots under `.scratch/work-items/<work-item>/<run>/<entry>`: metadata-only `retain` carries no proof and leaves the root untouched, while `delete` requires an exact same-item accepted pointer plus complete Git-object or accepted-artifact proof. The existing close owner rejects undeclared canonical namespaces, archives plus regenerates the README before deletion, and uses a deterministic same-parent tombstone for retry. The strict schema-owned decoder rejects duplicate keys and bounded-input violations on both writer and validator paths; Git batch proof now requires one well-formed blob-or-missing response per requested object. The leaking pull-request literal-command fixture is test-owned and self-cleaning. **Why it matters:** successful work can stop accumulating known disposable evidence while abnormal, ambiguous, retained, or unproven evidence remains recoverable and visible. **Preserved:** the watchdog and maintenance command stay read-only, legacy ledgers remain compatible only when no undeclared canonical namespace exists, existing and historical `.scratch/` are untouched, no sweeper/manifest/age-deletion rule or `.gitignore` change was added, provider capture behavior and hook registration identities remain unchanged, and Gemini/Qwen are untouched.

- **Codex and Claude review-loop bindings now expose one installed frozen-artifact state owner for their supported formal path.** `review_loop_state.py` atomically admits and reads back each round, binds every recorded result/failure/retry to that round's artifact revision, supports explicit V1 migration and rollback, and leaves the repository validator as a thin development/continuous-integration caller. Mutations use a stable file plus an operating-system exclusive kernel lock; graceful cancellation cleans uncommitted resources, while hard process termination is reconciled on the next invocation and corrupt/uncertain state fails with `RLSTATE_RECOVERY_REQUIRED` without deleting candidate evidence. **Why it matters:** operations that use the state owner cannot count an unpersisted round or silently mix reviews of different artifact bytes. **Open boundary:** `begin` is not an executable host-enforced dispatch gate, and no shipped mechanism observes a loop that bypasses it; the exact defect remains open at `work-items/bugs/2026-07-26-nothing-observes-a-review-loop-that-ran-without-a-ledger.md`. **Preserved:** provider completion, work-item ledger and scoped-invalidation semantics, operator-owned reviews, the three-round cap, publication/transcript handling, hooks, and deprecated Gemini/Qwen surfaces are unchanged.

- **Blocking and audit hooks now inspect one byte-bounded current-turn snapshot instead of reading an entire transcript and then keeping only 100 records.** The shared owner reads at most 8 MiB, applies the existing genuine-user boundary predicate, and returns typed non-success states without partial evidence; the publication gate, bug-fix discipline, passive-polling guard, and repository-orientation audit all use that owner. **Why it matters:** long sessions no longer make valid current-turn evidence unreachable merely because more than 100 records followed the user message, and large transcripts no longer impose whole-file reads on these paths. **Preserved:** publication authorization and strict pull-request history semantics, each hook's existing fail-open or fail-closed polarity, Codex/Claude mirror parity, and Gemini/Qwen behavior are unchanged. Dense full-process latency remains separately tracked and is not claimed to meet 150 ms.

## 2026-08-08

### Fixed

- **The always-loaded shared governance spine now has one current owner per invariant instead of overlapping rule cards.** Verification, completion, ownership/seam selection, canonical-text replacement, portable configuration, and resource lifecycle wording were consolidated in place; provider timing/configuration remains in the existing platform contracts, while formula and program-listing methodology remains with the applicable specialist skills. **Why it matters:** the installed spine is 34,799 UTF-8 bytes under the unchanged 39,900-byte cap, leaving 5,101 bytes of real headroom without hiding rules through minification or raising the limit. **Operator impact:** agents receive the same distinct safety and delivery obligations with less repeated context and clearer ownership. **Preserved:** direct external launch and no-silent-fallback rules, separate untrusted-execution and publication-sanitation boundaries, strict validator scopes, Codex/Claude routing behavior, and deprecated example-only Gemini/Qwen status are unchanged.

- **The agent-run ledger now refuses mutations outside the current active work-item root and exposes a pinned success marker.** `init` and `append` resolve their target before any write and accept only a direct `work-items/active/<item>` child; archived, nested, sibling, and symlink-resolved non-active paths fail without changing status, ledger, lock, or temporary files. The writer-owned append marker remains exactly `RESULT: PASS append` and is guarded independently from the full output line. **Why it matters:** a completed or archived task can no longer acquire new execution events accidentally, while automation can distinguish a durable append from a rejected or rolled-back attempt. **Preserved:** valid active-item writes, invalid-event rollback, read-only archive rollup, ledger schema, and `init` content semantics are unchanged.

- **Active work-items now have one durable execution record instead of mandatory per-lane report and plan copies.** A current task counts as active only when it has `work-items/active/<slug>/`; its specialists write their canonical artifacts there and the root keeps the concise lane result and provider provenance in `agent-runs.jsonl`. **Why it matters:** staged work remains recoverable without multiplying the same outcome across local report and plan directories. **Operator impact:** trivial work writes nothing; a meaningful standalone result with no active item may use one `.reports/` summary, and an explicitly requested standalone plan may use one `.plans/` snapshot. **Authorized common-skill update:** the parity-pinned VAK dissertation review wording was synchronously updated in the Codex, Claude, Gemini, and Qwen packs. **Preserved:** provider provenance remains durable, `.reports/`/`.plans/` path exemptions and existing local files remain untouched, and provider runtime, routing, installer, lifecycle, ledger, and hook behavior is unchanged.

- **Canonical staged work-item start now publishes a valid execution-ledger admission event with the active item.** The lifecycle owner builds one schema-version 2 completed `standalone` event through the existing ledger builder and serializer, then writes it beside `admission.md` and `status.md` before the single directory publication. **Why it matters:** a newly started staged item now passes strict state validation immediately instead of requiring a later manual ledger repair, while any build, serialization, write, temporary-validation, or final-publication failure restores the original candidate bytes. A ledger-helper load failure now reports the stable `WI-LEDGER-BOOTSTRAP-INVALID` lifecycle identifier without leaking a traceback. **Operator impact:** use the ordinary `mutate-work-item.py start` path; do not run `agent-run-ledger.py init` merely to make that staged item valid. **Preserved:** quick-fix items remain ledger-free until real delegated work requires a ledger, missing or empty staged ledgers still fail validation, cancellation and termination signals remain unswallowed, no specialist execution is fabricated, and the CLI and event schema are unchanged.

- **Claude force-mode installation now binds the Lead role through Claude's documented main-agent setting instead of relying only on reminder text.** When the effective `delegationMode` is `force` and the user-owned `settings.json` has no `agent` scalar, the Claude installer atomically writes `agent: "lead"`; the dual-safe Lead definition then uses its documented `initialPrompt: /lead` to load the one canonical Lead skill. **Why it matters:** new Claude sessions receive the actual main-agent role selection that the existing orchestration contract requires. **Operator impact:** an explicit non-Lead `agent`, higher-precedence managed/project selection, or `--agent` remains authoritative; `auto`, `manual`, and unresolved mode preserve the setting, and an already-running session needs a new session or explicit `--agent lead`. **Preserved:** stale `subagent_type: lead` dispatch still fails closed, unrelated settings and hooks survive reinstall, transaction rollback restores the original settings and Lead definition, and Codex, Gemini, Qwen, MCP-force, and typed-routing behavior are unchanged.

## 2026-08-03

### Fixed

- **Seven narrowly-scoped fixes now preserve their owning contracts instead of accepting false failures or misclassifying valid input.** `PostToolUse` registration is supported without dropping the default or caller-supplied tool matcher on platform shapes that support it; the work-item field parser accepts `*`-prefixed bullets; and the Lead guard recognizes literal `never invoke $lead`, `never launch $lead`, and equivalent refusals while continuing to reject positive Lead dispatch. The shared publication-command segmenter now consumes attached numeric file-descriptor redirections through its owned public-`shlex` prepass for both push and publication-scan consumers. **Why it matters:** installers, lifecycle checks, delegation guards, and the publication gate now evaluate the operator's actual input shape instead of failing or weakening the intended gate. **Preserved:** positional, spaced, quoted, escaped, embedded, and assignment-like `2` tokens remain ordinary arguments; heredoc handling and the generic range-credit grammar are unchanged; universal-hook behavior still has one canonical owner mirrored only through the existing synchronization gate, with no Gemini/Qwen change.

- **Meaning-changing document clarifications now replace stale prose instead of accumulating correction layers.** The existing shared anti-layering rule now requires one coherent current truth: when a clarification changes a live textual artifact's meaning, the prior wording is rewritten or removed and history stays in version control or a designated decision/closure record. Genuinely cumulative, non-conflicting additions remain additive. **Why it matters:** iterative drafting no longer turns current documentation into an expanding chronology of corrections. **Preserved:** the fix uses the existing shared owner and Codex/Claude validation seams; it adds no hook, registry, policy family, role copy, dependency, or Gemini/Qwen change.

- **Provider runs now have one bounded, self-cleaning result lifecycle instead of accumulating permanent capture directories.** The Claude-owned Python prompt wrapper creates a randomized private run directory, streams standard output and standard error through one combined byte budget, settles the child process and reader threads on success, failure, timeout, cancellation, and partial startup, then either removes the owned directory or preserves it as explicit non-success recovery evidence. Codex results are selected from strict UTF-8, line-feed-delimited JSON Lines output; Claude results use the same bounded supervisor. Exactly one escaped result envelope is flushed before the optional path-free terminal ledger event, so no durable `PASS` can precede delivery. Pytest now routes its cache under `/.scratch/` and documented test runs disable bytecode writes. **Why it matters:** ordinary main-conversation and delegated provider work no longer grows `.scratch/` without bound or drops build/test caches into the repository root, while abnormal runs remain diagnosable and cannot silently truncate output into a false success. **Preserved:** existing `.scratch/` evidence is not deleted, provider arguments/authentication and Codex trust preflight are unchanged, Codex keeps its own pack-local polling transport, shared governance remains transport-neutral, and deprecated Gemini/Qwen examples are untouched.

## 2026-08-02

### Changed

- **One explicit user grant can now cover repeated safe pushes for one concrete GitHub pull request (PR), without weakening the leak-check.** The exact genuine-user message `[approve-pr-publication:v1 pr=https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>/pull/<positive-number>]` creates an ephemeral transcript-scoped grant, and exact `[revoke-pr-publication:v1]` revokes it. On every attempted push the universal Codex/Claude gate freshly verifies the open PR, current base/head repository and ref binding, direct GitHub remote, remote object, default/protection state, and one exact ordinary `HEAD` refspec. Continuing authorization is intentionally limited to provider head names of 1–255 ASCII characters in `[A-Za-z0-9._/-]` that pass direct-argument `git check-ref-format --branch`, and to the owning shell's canonical literal serialization of the absolute resolved Git executable plus the exact push arguments. It also requires a new unused non-empty range receipt bound to the decoded remote, destination, and local `HEAD` tip; any ambiguity, drift, provider failure, unsafe/out-of-language destination, non-canonical command, stale/reused receipt, or active-route command mismatch denies without falling through to generic scan credit. **Why it matters:** a user no longer repeats approval after each review-driven commit to the same PR, while every actual publication still receives a fresh leak-check and current-state validation and Windows PowerShell 5.1 cannot reinterpret an admitted branch byte. **Preserved:** all-dry-run behavior, the one-turn `[approve-publication]` exception, subagent/non-push behavior, and the generic no-grant route remain unchanged except that a detected non-dry push with no readable transcript now fails closed. No grant state, service, config, scanner format, hook registration, installer identity, or Gemini/Qwen surface was added.

## 2026-08-01

### Changed

- **Orchestrarium now records Python as the sole owner of executable script logic on its Codex and Claude production surfaces.** A Bash (`.sh`) file may remain only as a thin unconditional launcher into its named Python sibling; PowerShell (`.ps1`) implementations and rollback copies are not retained. **Why it matters:** maintainers no longer have two shell-language decision paths that can drift across Windows and POSIX. **Operator impact:** when changing a production script, update the Python owner or remove the obsolete shell implementation instead of adding a second implementation. **Preserved:** this is repo-local source policy, not installed governance for target projects; the deprecated Gemini/Qwen example packs remain unchanged pending their separate distribution decision.

- **Codex hook installation and controlled non-interactive Codex launches now verify host-runnable trust, not only that a hook target can execute directly.** The health helper reconciles each live Orchestrarium registration with Codex `hooks/list`; installer VERIFY reports only complete registration identities changed by its own transaction as `PENDING_MANUAL_TRUST`—event, matcher, handler type, exact command, and exact registration source all participate—while pre-existing untrusted or modified registrations fail. After the interactive Trust action, and immediately before a repository-owned Codex prompt launch, `require` automatically consumes the installed helper's sibling generated inventory, accepts only host-reported `trusted` entries, and prevents launch on missing inventory, drift, or unavailable host inventory. **Why it matters:** a directly executable hook with a stale trust hash is silently omitted by Codex, so an earlier structural health check could report a disarmed installation as healthy. **Operator impact:** complete the existing interactive Trust step, then run the documented installed `check-hook-health.py --codex-trust-mode require` command before relying on automation. **Preserved:** hook registration shape and mutation ownership, Codex interactive Trust persistence, direct executable checks, Claude launch behavior, and Gemini/Qwen paths are unchanged.

## 2026-07-31

### Changed

- **Legacy directory-shaped backlog records now have truthful atomic transitions in the existing work-item lifecycle owner.** An admitted folder can become one generated-README-visible flat candidate while retaining every accepted source text, byte length, and SHA-256 digest as a labelled appendix; a never-admitted design can be rejected directly into the existing UTC-month archive with its original bytes, explicit product disposition, source hashes, and incoming-link inventory, without fabricated candidate, active, ledger, or closure history. Before the conversion commit point, failures restore the complete source folder and exact README bytes. After the byte-verified candidate and generated README commit, a cleanup fault truthfully preserves those committed bytes and leaves one owner-bound residue for same-command cleanup replay; it does not pretend the source folder was restored. The operator-authorized `terminalize-v1` repair now also uses the category-admission contract for every supported flat category, including roadmaps, instead of hard-coding bugs and decisions. **Why it matters:** maintainers can reconcile the pre-V1 backlog layout without manual moves, invisible candidates, invented development history, or cleanup errors that misrepresent committed state. **Operator impact:** use `convert-legacy-candidate` for an admitted legacy folder, retry the same command only for its reported owner-bound cleanup residue, use `retire-legacy-backlog` for an explicit product rejection, and keep using strict UTC plus the existing audit gate. **Preserved:** one lifecycle owner, one archive root, immutable archived identity, and Gemini/Qwen example surfaces remain unchanged.

- **Work-item lifecycle now has one physical, fail-closed owner on the production Codex and Claude lines.** Current records remain in their category roots and terminal identities move only to UTC-month archive paths after explicit category admission and terminal evidence; reopening creates a successor instead of reversing archived history. **Why it matters:** the generated README and compatibility index can no longer silently act as competing state stores, and a historical record with missing evidence stays visible for a human decision instead of acquiring an invented date, status, or destination. **Operator impact:** use `mutate-work-item.py audit` to obtain the inventory before any migration and resolve denied historical records with authoritative data.

- **The archival Stop adapter is retired from production installation.** Codex retains 12 hook registrations and Claude retains 13 (including its typed-routing audit); upgrade removes only the obsolete adapter/registration while preserving every remaining registration identity. **Why it matters:** lifecycle correctness now has one validated owner rather than a parallel Stop-path interpretation, so operators do not need a hook re-trust merely because the retired adapter disappeared.

## 2026-07-30

### Changed

- **Production installation and runtime maintenance now have one Python owner on the Codex and Claude lines.** The root router, provider installers, pack validators, publication checks, provider launch/watcher helpers, and installed task-memory helpers no longer ship or install PowerShell implementations; retained Bash files are thin launchers into the same Python logic, and reinstall reclaims only unchanged retired Orchestrarium-owned `.ps1` copies. **Why it matters:** Windows and POSIX now exercise the same decision logic instead of maintaining diverging PowerShell/Python implementations, while installed validation resolves source, extracted, and installed layouts explicitly and fails closed when an adapter cannot find its canonical engine. **Preserved:** user-owned files and customized Codex native overrides are not replaced, target `.gitignore` files remain untouched, and the four deprecated Gemini/Qwen PowerShell example files are unchanged pending the `npm-skillpack-distribution` decision.

- **Admitted `quick-fix` work now survives a process or machine crash without acquiring a heavyweight prelude.** Before the first repository mutation, the main conversation creates one minimal `work-items/active/<slug>/status.md` containing ordinary lifecycle data plus the task, current step, last result, and next action. **Why it matters:** interrupted execution can resume from durable state instead of losing the in-flight request. **Preserved:** no roadmap, brief, Research, Design, Plan, consultant, pre-implementation review, or report is required before that mutation; re-classification enriches the same item, direct single-specialist requests remain exempt unless explicitly admitted as `quick-fix`, and delivered work still closes and archives immediately.

- **Codex native role installation is now create-only in 1.x.** Fresh project and global installs create absent current role TOMLs, canonical skills, and Claude projections; the source manifest validates payload bytes but is never installed as an ownership receipt. Identical objects are no-ops, while changed bytes, type collisions, or reparse points fail without mutation.
