The cleanup also rewords the fail-closed `throw` to name the widened search (the install root, its parent, and the WSL-filtered `PATH`) and adds regression tests across all six launchers — a `subst`-synthesized drive-root git that must reach the `PATH` bash with no binding crash, a synthetic `mingw64`-layout fixture (a real standalone MSYS2 bash copied to the grandparent) covering the root fix on any host with a copyable bundled bash, and a nonstandard-layout git with an unrelated grandparent `bash.exe` that must be skipped (grandparent not probed, `PATH` bash selected). **Who this affects:** operators running the publication safety gate or either pack validator on a Windows host where `git` resolves through `mingw64\bin` and/or where WSL is installed and a `System32\bash.exe` or `Microsoft\WindowsApps\bash.exe` shadows Git Bash on `PATH`. **Preserved:** two-levels-up derived-root precedence, the non-WSL `PATH` fallback order, and fail-closed behavior when no usable shell exists are unchanged; the three `check-publication-safety.ps1` copies remain byte-identical mirrors. The example-only Gemini and Qwen pack validators (`src.gemini/scripts/validate-pack.ps1`, `src.qwen/scripts/validate-pack.ps1`) carry the same pattern but are deliberately left unchanged under the standing operator policy that they must never be globally installed (exemption recorded in `work-items/bugs/2026-07-19-gemini-qwen-validate-pack-ps1-shell-resolution.md`).

## 2026-07-18

### Fixed

- **Codex skill metadata default prompts now stay within the runtime interface limit.** Five shipped `interface.default_prompt` values were shortened to 981, 978, 909, 905, and 958 characters, and the Codex pack validator now parses all 45 `openai.yaml` files and fails with the file and length when any prompt exceeds 1,024 characters. This prevents non-empty metadata from being silently discarded by the Codex CLI loader; the Claude line is unchanged because it has no `openai.yaml` equivalent.

- **The Model Context Protocol (MCP) SessionStart reminder now generalizes connected-but-uninitialized handling to any server that requires an initialization step and states the codegraph lifecycle accurately.** When a connected server reports no index, the reminder tells main loops and dispatched workers to initialize it per the server's own instructions and then use or await the result; the example now names `codegraph init` for the initial index and its file-watcher for freshness, rather than implying that a query builds an unindexed graph. Only a genuinely absent server -- not connected, not installed, or absent from tool discovery -- may be skipped with an explanation.

- **Dispatched Codex reviews launched through the Claude-side Bash wrapper now preserve the reviewer's actual final verdict instead of losing it to an empty stdout trace or a main-conversation Stop-hook acknowledgement.** The Bash Codex prompt wrapper passes `--output-last-message` to `codex exec`, records the dedicated `.lastmsg` artifact, and prefers that file for the completion oracle and ledger evidence while retaining the full `.out` and `.err` captures for diagnostics and falling back to `.out` when no final-message artifact exists. The wrapper also marks the child process with `ORCHESTRARIUM_DISPATCHED_REVIEW=1`; both production Stop-hook brains treat that environment as a non-main-conversation context and fail open before applying passive-polling or work-item-archival discipline. **Who this affects:** operators using `invoke-codex-prompt.sh` for Codex-backed external review lanes now receive the anchored `GATE: PASS|REVISE` message in the verdict artifact and ledger even when Codex emits additional trace output or the repository contains a closed-but-unarchived work-item. The sibling PowerShell transport is outside this fix's explicitly approved Bash-only boundary. Ordinary main-conversation Stop-hook blocking, provider availability probing, trace capture, ledger settlement, completion-oracle checks, and wrapper exit-code propagation remain unchanged.

- **Follow-up hardening closes the remaining Claude-line dispatch gaps across Bash and PowerShell.** Both Codex wrappers now emit watcher commands resolved from their own script directories, and both Codex completion oracles prefer a non-empty `.lastmsg` for final-line classification, emptiness, and ledger evidence before falling back to `.out`. Both Claude prompt wrappers scope `ORCHESTRARIUM_DISPATCHED_REVIEW=1` to the dispatched Claude child, so a Claude-backed review cannot be contaminated by the main-conversation Stop-hook guards. The PowerShell watcher now reports argument failures on stderr with a Bash-equivalent usage error, accepts fractional polling without a zero-second busy-spin on Windows PowerShell 5.1, and exposes valid comment-based help. **Who this affects:** operators copying watcher commands from source-repo, global-install, or pack layouts and operators using either Windows or Bash dispatch transport. Existing artifact capture, ledger settlement, exit propagation, ordinary Stop-hook blocking, and the watcher’s terminal-condition precedence remain unchanged.

- **Protected or unclassified review findings now have the complete, fail-closed security-reviewer discharge boundary their accepted closure design required.** The validator accepts the typed `WAIVED:security-reviewer` gate only when the event passes every validation rule and all four authorization controls hold together: `security-reviewer` is the declared `role` or `assignedRole`; the normalized `executionRole` is reviewer-side (`internal` or `external-reviewer`); the event names a nonblank artifact whose resolved path exists; and every target is protected (`publication-safety` or `security`) or has no `findingClass`. Active validation and archived-item scanning now obtain the same explicit, position-aligned per-event validity results from the validator, so authorization is independent of attacker-controlled run identifiers (`runId`), human-readable diagnostic wording, and caller-specific validity reconstruction; skipped legacy archive positions remain quiet and ineligible. Security-reviewer waivers also preflight their complete target set before changing discharge state: every target identity must resolve uniquely to one earlier valid open position, and a mixed protected/non-protected set or duplicate identity rejects the whole relation without partially closing another obligation. A failed validation, identity, execution-role, artifact, target-scope, target-validity, or target-uniqueness dimension therefore cannot silently close its `REVISE` obligation. Artifact semantics intentionally remain exactly path-existence based, matching the existing `Path.exists()` contract; this change does not add regular-file, byte-size, content, or completion-marker requirements. The agent-run ledger producer remains unchanged: it still records caller-supplied provenance and validates a temporary candidate before atomic replacement, while the validator alone owns waiver authorization. The existing user boundary is preserved: `WAIVED:user` remains invalid for protected or unclassified findings, and its typed non-protected discharge behavior is unchanged. **Who this affects:** operators and security reviewers maintaining tracked review obligations gain one auditable exception path across active and archived validation that cannot be broadened by malformed event data, a spoofed or duplicated run identifier, diagnostic wording, partial target eligibility, an author-side or advisory execution lane, a missing artifact path, or a non-protected target class.

### Changed

- **The VAK dissertation-review skill body now explicitly includes `нормоконтроль` as formatting control and normative-compliance control across all four provider-pack mirrors.** This restores body truth with the front-loaded Codex description and the detailed review-dimensions reference while preserving the repository's common-skill body-parity contract, so production Codex and Claude Code reviewers receive the promised review scope after skill selection and the Gemini and Qwen example-provider mirrors stay methodologically aligned. The workflow remains advisory, and its existing review dimensions and qualification-decision boundary are unchanged.

- **The external-dispatch run-completion contract now states the completion-notification model explicitly, so a session can no longer strand a launched external CLI by waiting for a notification that its launch mode never delivers.** A recurring precedent: sessions launching an external CLI (for example Codex) either improvised the launch from memory instead of reading the contract, or ended the turn "waiting to be notified" of completion — but whether a harness completion notification arrives depends on HOW the run was launched, and an improvised or untracked launch delivers none, so the session stalls forever. **What changed:** `external-dispatch.md`'s `## Run-completion oracle` now records that a completion notification (or its absence) is never the oracle — the `.out` artifact shape and `.err` cleanliness are verified directly, regardless of any notification. Its `## Stall and timeout policy` on the Claude line names the three conjunctive conditions under which a harness notification actually fires: the run is launched by the MAIN orchestrating loop (a dispatched subagent is never re-invoked for a background child — cross-referencing the existing no-spawn-and-wait rule), as a harness-tracked background run (Claude Bash `run_in_background: true`), whose tracked process stays attached until the provider exits (a foreground launch that forks, `&`-detaches, or `nohup`s the provider notifies on the shell's exit, not the provider's). The canonical wrapper is the recommended way to satisfy the attachment condition plus the transport contract, but it is not itself the notifier. The Codex-line copy is worded per-provider: the Codex pack ships no primary-run wrapper and its transport-neutral chain provides no harness re-invoke, so active polling is stated as the only completion signal — the Claude `run_in_background` mechanism is deliberately NOT mirrored into the Codex contract, because it is a Claude-harness property that does not hold there. **Who this affects:** any operator or session dispatching an external CLI through either pack; the fix closes the "stranded waiting for a notification that never comes" failure by making the poll-don't-wait rule and its launch-mode dependency explicit at the canonical owner. **Preserved:** the existing "actively poll `.out`/`.err`/process status" instruction is unchanged; the every-turn turn-anchor reminder was intentionally NOT lengthened, because the passive-polling Stop hook already owns the passive-wait failure at the correct enforcement tier.

- **Claude-line Codex dispatches now ship a reusable active watcher instead of requiring a hand-rolled polling loop.** `await-codex-dispatch.sh` and its faithful PowerShell sibling perform one-shot polling for a non-empty final-message/output artifact, a changed commit, an idle error artifact, or a hard timeout, while treating missing files and failed commit probes as not-yet-terminal. Both `invoke-codex-prompt` wrappers print the exact watcher command wired to the current dispatch's `.out`, `.err`, and `.lastmsg` paths after their artifact paths, making active polling an operator-visible launch step rather than a reminder to reconstruct a loop. **Who this affects:** operators dispatching background Codex reviews through the Claude-line transport can recover terminal state after notification loss or session teardown without duplicating ad-hoc polling logic. The helper remains Claude-line-only, preserves the harness notification as non-authoritative, and does not change Codex-pack surfaces.

- **`check-work-items-state` can no longer be quoted as "all closed": a green result now prints every still-open active work-item with its Next action, and the RESULT line names itself "valid state only, NOT completion".** An operator caught a false-completion claim — a final summary asserted "all items closed" while two work-items were `active` with unstarted Next actions, leaning on a `RESULT: PASS` that only ever meant "no open ledger obligations + valid hygiene", never "done". The checker now prints, on every run with active items, `STILL OPEN - these active work-items are NOT closed (a PASS is valid state, not completion):` followed by each item and the first line of its `## Next action`, and the pass verdict became `RESULT: PASS - valid state only, NOT completion: N active work-item(s) STILL OPEN ...` (zero active items prints `no active work-items`). **Who this affects:** anyone — human or agent — who read the work-items state check as a completion signal; the conflation between "valid state" and "all work done" is now removed at the tool surface. Because the enumeration prints arbitrary status-file content (Next-action previews often contain em-dashes/arrows), the tool's stdout/stderr streams are set at entry to replace un-encodable characters, so the report cannot crash with a `UnicodeEncodeError` on a non-UTF-8 console (e.g. cp866) — the encoding guard is owned at the stream, not by sanitizing one string. Regression-tested, including a non-ASCII Next action under a forced narrow console encoding. The deeper behavioural guard (flagging a done-claim asserted while `work-items/active/` still holds work) is tracked as a follow-up in `work-items/bugs/`.

- **The spine's mandatory session-logging rule no longer primes premature stops: writing a session log is now a finished-unit record, not a completion signal that licenses ending the turn while work remains.** An opus audit measured a contradiction: the spine's `Session logging rule` (`shared/AGENTS.shared.md:25`) MANDATED writing a `.reports/` session log carrying a `PASS/REVISE/BLOCKED` outcome "whenever the session produced a result", while the delegation-principles line (`:56`) forbids a "final-style summary/'what next?' while a plan or known next action remains" — so the pack mandated the complete-feeling wrap-up-with-verdict that its own anti-stop rule forbids, and producing it primed the stop (the salience analysis put the anti-stop clause at char 471 of 548, the least-salient position). The rule is reworded within the spine's hard size cap (net −1 byte, `39,898 → 39,897` of `39,900`) to preserve every obligation — main-conversation-and-subagents scope, all three triggers (result / routing decision / completed review), the mandatory `MUST`, the `.reports/YYYY-MM/` destination — while adding the reconciliation: the log records a finished unit, not completion, and work continues when a next action or plan item remains. **Who this affects:** any participant (main conversation or subagent) writing session logs; the log is still always required at each unit boundary — only its false pull toward ending the turn is removed. The one-paragraph-summary-with-outcome requirement on the next line is unchanged. Reviewed for substance preservation (no obligation gutted, 111/111 protection tokens intact) by an independent spine review-loop, per the standing rule that the size validator passes a kept-byte-but-gutted rule and only a review loop catches substance loss.

- **Reviewer findings now distinguish inline fixes from design decisions, and review closure now makes HOW-to-verification independence explicit.** Every new architecture, security, performance, user-experience, and accessibility review finding on the Claude Code and Codex production lines carries a per-finding `fix-class: {inline-sufficient | design-decision}` tag plus a separately labeled, non-binding advisory HOW. Six inlined escalation triggers send underdetermined or high-risk fixes through the correct design or constraint owner and a separate `/agents-review-loop` fix-design pass, while a one-way ratchet permits `inline-sufficient` → `design-decision` escalation but never downgrade. Inline-sufficient fixes do not gain a new pre-implementation HOW-review pass, and their existing loop-to-PASS re-verification remains mandatory. For design-decision fixes that followed reviewer-authored HOW, at least one discharging verdict must come from an angle that did not author that HOW; otherwise the result stays `UNVERIFIED`. **Who this affects:** every reviewer and review-loop run on the two production packs gains deterministic fix routing without moving acceptance authority from WHAT to advisory HOW. **Preserved:** the WHAT evidence object, S4 verdict vocabulary, loop-to-PASS obligations, distinct-engine anti-layering lane, persisted ledgers, role count, and example-provider surfaces remain unchanged; the lagging Russian methodology closure section is tracked as a separate translation-sync follow-up.

## 2026-07-17

### Changed

- **The repo-local `.scratch/` janitor is reframed from a quarantine engine into a read-only lingering-valuables watchdog, ships as a new SessionStart hook installed by BOTH production packs, and gates on git-content-uniqueness rather than age after live-tree evidence showed the age-only cut had near-zero precision.** The operator reset the tool's purpose after the prior quarantine design (2026-07-16 entry below): they never wanted automated deletion -- they wanted `.scratch/` WATCHED so valuable data does not linger long enough to be accidentally overwritten, with the agent noticing on its own rather than a command they have to remember to run. **What changed:** `scripts/maintenance/cleanup.py`'s entire sweep/quarantine/restore/purge/`.hold`/manifest/digest mutation engine is deleted; it is replaced by a small, pure, read-only `scan_valuables()`. Its first cut gated purely on age (>7 days); an adversarial pass sampled the resulting flags against this repository's own live `.scratch/` and found 58 of 59 resolvable files were byte-identical to a blob already in the git object history -- recoverable, not actually at risk, and an 8631-file flat dump nobody would read. The PRIMARY predicate is now git-content-uniqueness: a candidate must be a non-empty, non-junk file whose exact bytes are NOT already recoverable from the repository's git object database (checked with two read-only subprocesses, `git hash-object` -- never `-w` -- and `git cat-file --batch-check`; verified to write nothing to `.git/objects`), leaving age as a severity/sort key (newest-modified first) instead of a filter. A narrow, evidence-based junk denylist (editor/OS litter, cache/dependency directories restricted to direct children of `.scratch/`, known external-CLI prompt-capture shapes for both `codex-prompts/` and `claude-prompts/`) remains a secondary filter, biased to over-warn. When `.scratch/` sits outside a git repository, git is unavailable, or any git call fails, the scan fails open to the original age-gated behavior rather than going silent or noisy. The walker also closes a Windows-specific gap: `DirEntry.is_symlink()` alone does not detect an NTFS directory junction, so a walker relying on it alone could descend into one and enumerate files outside `.scratch/`; the walker now also checks `os.path.isjunction` (with a stat-attribute fallback pre-3.12). A new universal SessionStart hook, `check-scratch-valuables` (shipped byte-identically in the pack-neutral canon and both the Claude Code and Codex production packs, auto-registered by all four production installers on the same no-matcher SessionStart family as `mcp-usage-reminder`), runs this scan every session start/resume/clear/compact and injects a context line ONLY when candidates are found -- byte-silent otherwise. When the candidate count is large, the injected message summarizes by top-level `.scratch/` subdirectory and lists a short window LED BY the longest-lingering files (the genuine accidental-overwrite risk) plus the newest-modified, instead of a flat dump, so it stays actionable regardless of how much has accumulated. The CLI on `scripts/maintenance/cleanup.py` remains only as a manual debugging aid for this repository; it performs no mutation either, and is not the "automatic" mechanism. **Who this affects:** operators of this monorepo, and any project installing the Claude Code or Codex pack, get a standing reminder for files worth rescuing that git cannot already recover, and can no longer accidentally lose data to an automated move/purge, because no code path in this tool writes, moves, or deletes anything -- on the live repository this collapsed the flagged set from 8631 (age-only) to 2261 (git-unique; the remainder is dominated by this repository's own long-running, largely-uncommitted review/QA history, not tool noise), formatted as a readable directory summary instead of a flat dump. **Governance:** binding design is `work-items/active/2026-07-16-cleanup-routine/design-watchdog-reframe.md`; the deleted mutation engine's own decision record (`2026-07-16-cleanup-quarantine-policy`) is superseded by this reframe.

- **A clean architecture verdict that relies on C1 must RE-AIM the single-owner assessment at adjacent objects and prove it with a mandatory record.** A lens can be present, sharp and productive and still miss an entire class because it was never pointed at a second axis: a real audit ran the split-ownership lens, found "one conceptual decision has four independent owners", and never found the same defect in policy VALUES — it asked who owns the decision, never who owns the number. The rule for the number already existed (law C1 names "a shared constant … a calibration table") and was simply not applied, so this ships as a forcing function rather than more letter. **What changed:** when a clean/verified verdict relies on a C1 (single-owner-invariant) assessment, the dispatch and returned artifact require an `Object-axis record` — one row per C1 assessment (`lens | primary object examined | adjacent object classes re-aimed at | decision facts proved, not a proxy | result + evidence`); an absent or empty-celled record fails mechanical acceptance exactly like the existing receiving-side echo. Verdicts that do not rely on C1 owe no Object-axis record. The adjacent axis is defined FUNCTIONALLY, never as a noun checklist (a closed list of nouns is the same blind lens one level down): the representations a legitimate policy change must update together, objects under another owner that the operation touches, and the facts the decision actually used rather than a proxy for them. **Noise guard:** a value candidate is admitted only BEFORE searching and only when it names the policy owner, the triggering change, the consumers that must co-vary, and the one place they would look — a world fact, protocol constant, or algorithm-local literal fails that test and stays exempt by design, and a literal is a discovery seed, never a finding; without co-variation it is not a single-owner defect at all but plain hardcoding, a different rule with a different fix. **Who this affects:** anyone dispatching or returning an architecture review that relies on a C1 assessment through either pack, including external review adapters; Quality Assurance, security, usability, accessibility, performance, and other non-C1 review gates incur no new record obligation. The trigger lives in `subagent-contracts.md` because the reviewer role does not reach the external-dispatch lane, where the motivating misses happened. **Preserved:** the class-completeness trigger it sits beside is unchanged and explicitly distinct (that one fires after a finding; this one before a clean C1 verdict). No literal-hunting validator was added: a validator can assert a heading exists but cannot judge policy ownership, so it would buy noise and false confidence. Governing decision: `2026-07-17-object-axis-trigger`.

- **New project status-board convention + a precedent-driven orchestrator-upgrades ledger, and the review loop gains a model-mismatch trigger.** `work-items/README.md` is now a required, DERIVED status board (a scannable whole-project snapshot with a five-state glyph vocabulary — delivered/in-progress/not-started/parked/blocker) contracted in the `$lead` role (×4 providers) with a `$knowledge-archivist` per-wave Board-refresh duty; `work-items/orchestrator-upgrades.md` is a thin precedent→change→status promotion ledger (single-owner status, points rather than restates for non-shipped rows, no self-cert on ✅). The `operating-model.md` periodic-controls table gains Board-refresh + ledger-reconcile rows. The review-loop skill (`review-loop.md` Claude + Codex) gains a **model-mismatch trigger**: when the same defect class recurs across ~3 non-converging correctness rounds, stop correctness rounds and dispatch a model-review lane fed the manifestation phase-graph. **Who this affects:** orchestrators get a grounded whole-project view and a rule that catches a wrong MODEL instead of chasing manifestations. Reviewed to convergence (independent architecture + Codex round-3 PASS).

- **Review-loop discipline hardening from live incidents: review a frozen snapshot, read the ledger back before reporting, and never lose a verdict quietly.** `review-loop-methodology.md` gains "review a frozen artifact, never the live working tree" and "read the ledger back before reporting a verdict" (a `.out` prose is evidence of what a reviewer said, not that the obligation moved). The four prompt wrappers (`invoke-{claude,codex}-prompt.{sh,ps1}`) now make a dropped terminal-ledger write a LOUD failure naming the exact recovery command instead of a passing WARN. `validate-work-item-state.py` gains `repo_root_for()` so a reviewer's verdict on a REPOSITORY file (not only a work-item-local copy) is recordable. **Who this affects:** review loops on tracked work-items no longer silently drop a reviewer's verdict or accept a patch against a moving target.

- **Two universal reminder hooks (`turn-anchor-reminder`, `check-mcp-momentum`) are now shipped byte-identically into both production packs and registered by all four installers, and the whole hook suite was hardened against a strong-model audit.** turn-anchor (UserPromptSubmit) re-shows the continue-until-blocked anchor; check-mcp-momentum (PreToolUse) nudges toward MCP code-intelligence on search commands. A three-model audit (opus/fable/Codex) of every installed hook then fixed real defects: the passive-polling probe-allowance was dead (its turn-slice broke on a Claude Code tool_result entry, so a diligent probe-then-wait was blocked) — now tool_result-aware; the three warn-only AUDIT hooks (`machine-local-path`, `no-trash-in-repo`, `stale-relation-residue`) returned exit 0 on a hit, whose stderr is debug-log-only and invisible, so their warnings never surfaced — they now exit 1 (a non-blocking transcript notice, never exit 2) with the wrappers propagating the code but still failing open on wrapper error; the `no-trash-in-repo`/`git-push-gate`/`repository-orientation` matchers omitted `PowerShell` (this operator's primary Windows shell) so `git worktree add` never reached them — now added; `bugfix-discipline`'s doc-promised file:line discipline signal was missing from its regex (a plain-language diagnosis with a `foo.c:88` citation was wrongly denied) — now honored; `work-items-archival` did not match the canonical `- **Primary task status**:` marker and walked past the repo root into parent projects (blocking every session in a nested project on a parent's orphan) — both fixed, plus a done-word-followed-by-`when/if/когда` criterion exclusion. **Who this affects:** operators stop hitting false denials from their own diligent turns, warn-only audits become visible, and Windows worktree hygiene is actually enforced.

- **The bugfix-discipline PreToolUse hook no longer fires on a compaction-continuation prompt.** `hook_common.is_compact_summary()` keys on the `isCompactSummary`/`isMeta` envelope flags, and `extract_user_typed_text` returns empty for a compaction summary, so the guard no longer misreads the model's own post-compaction resume turn as a user bug report and denies its first edit. **Who this affects:** long sessions that compact mid-work stop tripping a false bugfix-discipline deny on resume.

## 2026-07-16

### Changed

- **New repo-local housekeeping engine (`scripts/maintenance/cleanup.py`, phase 1 of the cleanup routine): a dry-run-by-default janitor that quarantines stale `.scratch/` artifacts instead of deleting them.** Sweep eligibility is ONE rule — older than 7 days AND not live-referenced — where a reference is the artifact's repo-relative path, a separator-aware glob/brace citation, or the wrapper sibling-triple (`<stem>.md/.out/.err` pin as a set); a citation counts only while its work-item is active or its report is under 90 days, and any artifact past 90 days sweeps regardless. Nothing is ever hard-deleted from the live tree: files move to `.scratch/_trash/<date>/<run>/<original-path>` — a run namespace atomically reserved by exclusive `mkdir`, so collisions cannot occur and filenames are never mangled — and only that quarantine (inside the operator-declared wipeable zone) purges after its own 7-day restore window. State is the layout itself, so recovery is a plain re-scan; a fail-closed run-lock (no stale takeover, released on every path) excludes concurrent janitors. The engine also exposes `eligible --path <p> --json` as the single eligibility owner for future callers, reports work-item staleness (14-day horizon, informational only — never a gate), and prints per-run telemetry (eligible/pinned/blocked volumes, pinned-set size, age histogram). **Who this affects:** operators of this monorepo can now see, in a dry run, exactly what housekeeping would move and why; the engine is repo-local and is NOT installed into provider packs in this phase. **Governance:** the binding contract is the accepted decision record `2026-07-16-cleanup-quarantine-policy`; the design passed a three-angle review loop, and the mandated live-tree dry-run then earned its keep by exposing two grammar defects no design angle caught — markdown emphasis (`**bold**`) being read as the glob `**` (fnmatch matching everything), and bare-basename matching pinning 6531 files/80MB of scratch copies of real repo files plus prose-word collisions (`HEAD`, `config`). Both are fixed with falsifiers; measured live yield went from 226 files/6MB eligible to 8208 files/154MB, with the pinned set falling to 540 files/3.3MB.

- **A returned review `REVISE` now has a mechanical closure rule: it discharges only on a re-verification `PASS` from the same angle (or a recorded equivalent), a typed user waiver, or the security-reviewer exception — never on author belief or a green validator.** The always-on spine gains the 139-character closure clause (funded by the same-day audited compaction). The agent-run ledger schema moves to v2 (additive): `eventKind` launch/terminal lifecycle with `launchRunId` linkage, the `closesRunIds` closure relation, `lane`, typed `effort`, `artifactRevision`, and `findingClass` (a closed enum where `publication-safety`/`security` are non-user-waivable). The work-item validator enforces the closure rules (earlier-open-unique target; artifact+lane+authority+same-provider-tier on PASS closers; typed `WAIVED:user` shape) with rule-fire telemetry and a candidate-ledger seam; `check-work-items-state` fails on open v2 obligations by default, and the root publication gate blocks on them before every early-success path. The ledger writer becomes a kill-safe old-or-new transaction (fail-closed lock without stale takeover, temp-file + atomic replace, candidate validated before swap). All four prompt wrappers gain `--ledger`: the dispatch itself records launch and terminal events, settled by a shared completion oracle (final non-blank output line must be exactly `GATE: PASS|REVISE`, clean stderr, exit 0 — else the run records as blocked). **Who this affects:** orchestrators get the loop-to-PASS discipline enforced mechanically on tracked work-items (the motivating incident — closing a reviewer's REVISE after fixes plus a green validator, without re-dispatch — is now caught by a replay test); pre-existing v1 ledgers stay valid and migrate by hand. **Preserved/deferred:** per the step-back gate, the anti-laundering superstructure (anchors, TOCTOU digests, dispatcher-vector immutability, protectedLane, cross-provider maps) is deliberately deferred until telemetry or an incident funds it.

- **`externalCodexProfile`'s alternate Codex tier migrated from the volume model `gpt-5.6-luna` to the balanced model `gpt-5.6-terra`; `gpt-5.6-luna` is dropped from the enum.** The `gpt-5.6` Codex family is three-tier — `gpt-5.6-sol` (flagship) / `gpt-5.6-terra` (balanced) / `gpt-5.6-luna` (volume). The schema `externalCodexProfile.allowed` list previously offered the volume tier as the cheaper alternate profile; it now offers the balanced tier (`gpt-5.6-terra`, `model_reasoning_effort = "high"`) instead. terra is a genuine cheaper-and-faster-than-`gpt-5.6-sol-xhigh` reasoning lane (a distinct model, not an effort suffix on sol) that stays review-gated like any external lane — not a volume/overflow tier, so the previous luna-specific "bounded mechanical overflow / fast-volume / mandatory heavier-tier verification / Sonnet-equivalent" framing is replaced accordingly across the schema, defaults, presets, the four provider packs, docs, and the contract test. The `max-speed` preset, which pinned the volume tier, now pins `gpt-5.6-terra` — the lightest remaining Codex profile. **Routing evidence:** per the standing model-family-migration rule in both external-dispatch contracts, this Codex-family change keeps the shipped lane priorities `ASSUMPTION (UNVERIFIED)` pending re-benchmark, and a dated invalidation note was added to the 2026-05-01 routing-evidence document. **Who this affects:** operators who select the cheaper Codex profile now get the balanced reasoning model instead of the volume model; any repo-local `.agents-mode.yaml` still naming `gpt-5.6-luna` as a live value should update it to `gpt-5.6-terra`. **Preserved:** `default`, `gpt-5.6-sol-xhigh` (the shipped default), and `gpt-5.6-sol-max` are unchanged; the fallback-eligibility policy (only an explicitly configured fully-autonomous low-reasoning worker lane may auto-downgrade to the cheaper tier after usage-limit or quota exhaustion) is unchanged — only its target model is now terra.

## 2026-07-14

### Changed

- **Codex `SessionStart` reminders now emit the documented structured context envelope instead of bracket-led plain text.** Codex 0.144.4 treats output beginning with `[` or `{` as JSON-like and rejects it when it is not valid hook JSON, so the previous `mcp-usage-reminder` and `agents-mode-reminder` outputs—both beginning with `[`—each produced an `invalid session start JSON output` failure. Both Codex reminders now emit `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"SessionStart","additionalContext":"..."}}`, preserving the reminder text while removing the two noisy startup and post-compaction failures. The universal Model Context Protocol (MCP) reminder remains byte-identical across the Codex and Claude Code packs because Claude Code accepts the same envelope; Claude's provider-specific agents-mode reminder remains unchanged. Hook registration, trust, and delegation-mode resolution are unchanged. **Who this affects:** Codex operators receive both reminders as context without duplicate startup noise, while Claude Code operators retain their existing provider-specific behavior.

- **Reported runtime-performance symptoms now require a live profile of the exact reported scenario before audit-driven design or fixes.** The always-loaded Pre-fix gate treats unprofiled code audits and stale performance reports—including hedged "candidates to measure" findings—as advisory hypotheses rather than roots or fix gates, rejects proxy scenarios, and requires usage-based redesigns to combine profile evidence with an explicit domain or usage observation. Performance Engineer and Performance Reviewer contracts across all four provider packs consume the same preserved profile, re-profile only when it is absent or mismatched, and distinguish correctable missing evidence from verified external blockers. The Codex Lead operating model applies this precondition to every relevant route without changing role order, while prospective performance modeling without a reported runtime symptom remains allowed. The spine validator now pins and negative-tests all three enforcement teeth. **Who this affects:** orchestrators and performance roles must profile the user's actual slowdown first and may not build or block a fix on code reasoning alone.

## 2026-07-13

### Added

- **Repository orientation is now a mandatory pre-side-effect gate across the installed Claude Code and Codex packs.** The always-loaded shared spine requires a cited task-scoped record of repository status, canonical workflow, and protected surfaces before the first run, build, or mutation in an unfamiliar scope; names, file counts, recency, and layout are explicitly rejected as liveness evidence, and missing or conflicting authority blocks side effects pending owner or user resolution. Both provider Bootstrap sections operationalize the same `REPOSITORY ORIENTATION:` record as step 0. A new universal warn-only PreToolUse audit, shipped byte-identically in both production packs and auto-registered by the production installers, checks risky edits and repository-local run/build/test commands for a valid assistant-authored record, skips discovery and local artifact writes, and adds a high-confidence warning for archive/deprecated/superseded/frozen path segments. The hook deliberately never scans repository prose or infers canon from deprecation words; it always allows and fails open, so the text rule remains the authority. **Who this affects:** agents must prove they are operating on the live, mutable, repository-owned surface before execution, while operators retain non-blocking telemetry and existing hook opt-out behavior.

## 2026-07-11

### Added

- **Role best-practice upgrade Batch 4 completes the reviewer and external-lane contract upgrade across the Claude Code and Codex packs.** Architecture, security, performance, accessibility, and user-experience reviewers now bind approval to enumerated surfaces, one verdict per upstream claim or designed state, reproducible probes, explicit severity or standards thresholds, tracked non-passing findings, and bounded re-review procedures while preserving the existing P8 shared-state pin and independent domain gates. Each production pack's external-dispatch contract now owns its prompt governance, run-completion evidence, effort-tiered stall handling, availability-probe evidence binding, canonical execution record, and lane-specific write capability; external worker and reviewer adapters cite their pack owner, retain fail-closed provider behavior, and add reviewable edit handback, read-only review execution, anchored findings, and orchestrator verification of external verdicts. Consultant stall guidance now cites the shared contract instead of maintaining separate timeout numbers. **Who this affects:** reviewers get decidable PASS/REVISE/BLOCKED criteria, external operators can distinguish complete runs from truncated output, and both production packs share the same obligations without changing the frozen spine, operating-model contracts, or example-provider roles.

- **Role best-practice upgrade Batch 3 makes implementation and verification contracts executable across the Claude Code and Codex packs.** Backend, frontend, data, platform, toolchain, Qt user-interface, model/view, geometry, graphics, and visualization implementers now return the shared receiving-side evidence echo and apply role-specific floors for contracts, authorization, accessibility, migrations, reproducibility, Qt thread and signal correctness, geometric robustness, rendered-output inspection, and truthful visual encoding. Quality Assurance and Qt user-interface verification now account for incomplete suites and unverified states, preserve counted run evidence, prove new tests fail on pre-change behavior, classify flakes separately, exercise theme/scale/focus matrices, and persist non-passing findings. The four-provider Windows desktop manual-testing workflow now uses provider-neutral image inspection wording, probes modern desktop capture with a safe fallback, records cursor and display-scaling context, anchors every observation to evidence, supports machine pixel diffs, and persists non-passing visual findings. **Who this affects:** implementers receive concrete self-verification gates, reviewers receive reproducible artifacts instead of prose-only claims, and Windows desktop testers get the same body-verified workflow on Claude Code, Codex, Gemini, and Qwen while the shared spine and operating-model contracts remain frozen.

- **Role best-practice upgrade Batch 2 makes research, design, planning, product and user-experience guidance, and specialist constraints executable across the Claude Code and Codex packs.** Analyst memos now separate runtime proof, static reads, and unresolved assumptions; preserve independent hypotheses for independent symptoms; expose searched-and-excluded surfaces, repository-history risks, and a bounded saturation stop. Architect packages now own diff-invisible invariants and named regression guards, migration and compatibility strategy, observable failure discriminators, fresh research citations, and complete owner/probe claim triples; planners distribute those invariants into phases with exact verification commands, degenerate-result-resistant acceptance criteria, revert groups, and explicit integration ownership. Product Analyst and User Experience Designer artifacts now trace intent and scope to typed evidence, measurable success signals, state and accessibility matrices, visual anchors, numbered acceptance guidance, and observable flow completion. Security, performance, reliability, algorithm, and computational-science constraint owners now emit architect-shaped numbered claims with executable falsifying probes plus domain-specific threat, measurement, service-level, correctness-oracle, units, stability, and verification/validation floors. **Who this affects:** downstream implementers and reviewers receive producer-owned evidence and test oracles instead of inventing them at dispatch or gate time; provider-specific preambles remain intact, and the frozen shared spine, operating-model contracts, and example-provider role files are unchanged.

- **Role best-practice upgrade Batch 1 makes orchestration handoffs and gate artifacts directly falsifiable across the Claude Code and Codex packs.** The shared subagent contracts now require the receiving artifact to echo regression-guard results, diff-invisible invariant verdicts, defect-class coverage, evidence discipline, named tool surfaces, and the chosen model/effort; Lead records dispatch economics, preserves in-flight effort, rejects prose-only root-cause confirmation, and closes delivery against Product Manager success signals. Product Manager admissions now carry measurable success signals, force-ranked priority, evidence labels, and kill/re-intake triggers, with a Codex-specific in-context admission-independence guard. Knowledge Archivist now has an executable publication-approval contract, old-name residue sweeps, hook-aligned archive reconciliation, tier deduplication, and per-surface canonical-owner citations. Consultant recommendations now state what evidence would reverse them, disclose consumed inputs and prompt anchoring, and verify that non-interactive review output actually landed between explicit markers; the existing untrusted-continuation boundary remains intact. **Who this affects:** leads get stronger dispatch and acceptance evidence, product owners get outcome-accountable admissions, publication approvers get a concrete leak gate, and operators get aligned Batch 1 consultant and handoff-evidence expectations on both production packs without changing the frozen shared spine or operating-model contracts.

- **Trust-boundary hardening (audit Wave E): a blocking git-push publication gate, a layer-provenance trust gate for repo-supplied executable config, and an untrusted-data rule for consultant continuation prompts.** (1) `git push` — the highest-stakes irreversible action the pack governs — previously had zero structural enforcement while lower-stakes edit/stop moments had blocking hooks. A new blocking PreToolUse hook (`check-git-push-gate.py` + `.sh`/`.ps1` wrappers, shipped in the pack-neutral canon and both production packs, auto-registered by all four production installers on a `Bash` matcher) detects `git push` in command position with the shared shell-aware parser (quoted-string `git push` is data; `git push --dry-run` always allowed) and denies unless the LAST GENUINE USER MESSAGE carries the per-turn override `[approve-publication]` — honored ONLY from the user's own message, never from assistant prose or tool output — or the user's last message contains an explicit push instruction AND the current turn's model tool calls show a publication-safety scan invocation. It skips subagent contexts and fails open on any error; it is documented everywhere as a BACKSTOP for the human-review-before-push rule, not a replacement (a push hidden behind a wrapper script or `eval` is not modelled). Behavioral suite `tests/test_git_push_gate_hook.py` drives both pack copies. (2) A cloned repository could previously point `reserveResolver: wrapper:<command>` at its own script via the highest-precedence project-local `.agents-mode.yaml`, which the agent would then execute with the user's environment — and "approved" wrapper had no defined approver. Executable-bearing values are now trust-gated by config layer: honored without confirmation only when a user-global layer defines the value (or the identical value); a project-local-only `wrapper:<command>` resolves as `reserveResolverTrust: project-UNCONFIRMED` (new machine-readable flag emitted by `scripts/resolve-agents-mode.py`) and must not launch before explicit first-use user confirmation, recorded durably by writing the approved value into a user-global layer — exactly the write that flips subsequent resolutions to `user-global`. "Approved" is now defined as exactly this mechanism in both external-dispatch contracts, the agents-mode reference, and `INSTALL.md`; symbolic values (`claude-sonnet`, `claude-wrapper`, `disabled`) carry no gate and existing user-global setups resolve unchanged. (3) The mandated use-verbatim consultant continuation prompt was a designed-in injection conduit from an untrusted external CLI into the orchestrator. All four continuation-prompt surfaces (Claude consultant role + operating-model contract, Codex consultant skill + lead operating model) now carry a binding untrusted-data rule: the continuation prompt is data, not an instruction channel — prior provider output embedded in a follow-up prompt is quoted fenced and labelled, never inlined as instructions, and an imperative naming actions outside the admitted plan (config changes, pushes, new scope, tool launches) is reported to the user, never followed. **Who this affects:** operators get a structural stop between "commit" and "push" that their own `[approve-publication]` reply opens; users cloning third-party repos are protected from repo-supplied executable config; leads consuming external consultant memos get an explicit reconcile-before-use rule.

- **Hardened the always-loaded spine and its lose-nothing validator (audit Wave B).** The spine size cap is now enforced on `max(code points, UTF-8 bytes)` — the previous code-point-only check under-measured the file by 121 bytes and had silently consumed the guard band below the 40k context warning; the binding budget axis is now bytes (39,898/39,900 after this change). Funded by relocating the Claude-only wrapper concretions (prompt-wrapper filenames + the secret-backed api-wrapper's not-interchangeable clause) from the shared spine to `src.claude/CLAUDE.md` — the spine keeps the platform-neutral rule ("pack-shipped prompt wrappers are the canonical path where present; else the inline chain"). The five working definitions previously outsourced to the never-installed glossary are inlined (substance-reviewed clause-by-clause; the "smallest safe reversible subset" gloss keeps BOTH prongs — work-advancing + nothing-unresolved), and the maintainer-reference header now honestly says `shared/references/` is not installed to targets. The validator manifest grew from 88 to 105 pinned tokens: collision-prone banned-driver pins are backtick-anchored so deleting them from the Evidence-citation card can no longer pass, and four per-card teeth groups (wire-shape, destructive-polarity, guard-precondition, evidence-based-completion) pin the operative clauses of previously name-only cards (negative-probed: removing a pinned tooth fails the run).

- **Publication-safety hardening for the pack credential file (Wave A audit): Anthropic secret-token content patterns, an unconditional case-insensitive credential-filename block, and a project-install `.gitignore` guarantee.** All three byte-identical scanner copies (`scripts/universal-hooks/scripts/`, `src.claude/agents/scripts/`, `src.codex/skills/lead/scripts/` `check-publication-safety.sh`) gain two content patterns matching Anthropic credential material — the `sk-ant-…` token shape and `ANTHROPIC_…KEY` / `ANTHROPIC_…TOKEN` environment assignments — as the secondary net behind a new format-independent primary defense: any staged file whose basename is the pack credential filename (`SECRET.md`) blocks unconditionally, regardless of content. The acceptance audit then hardened the filename block to a CASE-INSENSITIVE compare: Windows, the pack's primary platform, has a case-insensitive filesystem, so a staged `secret.md` / `Secret.md` is the same credential file — the initial case-sensitive compare was a probe-confirmed bypass (both casings passed the gate) and every casing now blocks identically. Independently, project-level Claude installs (`install-claude.sh` + `.ps1`) now guarantee `/.claude/SECRET.md` — the `invoke-claude-api` wrapper's repo-local lookup candidate — is present in the target repo `.gitignore`, so the credential file cannot become trackable in the first place; a prose mention of the token prefixes (no value, no assignment) stays publishable. **Who this affects:** operators staging pack changes get the credential file blocked at the gate in every casing and on every platform; fresh project installs get the ignore entry automatically.

### Fixed

- **Stale-doc sweep + housekeeping (audit Wave G): five docs now state the five-role curated exception, the qwen runtime tables assert only shipped behavior, the cross-pack sync manifest anchors to headings instead of dead line numbers, and two consumer-less surfaces are deleted.** Five doc surfaces (`src.claude/README.md`, root `README.md`, `INSTALL.md`, `docs/new-session-guide.md`, `docs/provider-runtime-layouts.md`) described Lead as the ONLY role whose canonical contract lives under `skills/` — behind the shipped five-role model; all five now state the curated set (`lead` plus the four duals `product-manager`, `analyst`, `architect`, `planner`) matching `CLAUDE.md` `## Role definitions`, and `src.claude/README.md` gains the missing `skills/` source-surface bullet. The qwen runtime-layout section drops the `Extension-provided agents` row and the manifest-fields claim naming an `agents` payload the qwen extension does not ship (roles are skills-only; the manifest declares context file, skills, commands, and MCP servers). `cross-pack-reconciliation.md` replaces every line-number pointer (dead since the first edit above them) with exact `##`/`###` heading anchors under a stated anchor convention — line-number pointers are now banned in the manifest, renaming an anchored heading requires updating its row in the same change, and every anchor is verified against the four contract files. The delivered 2026-05-03 execution-tracking plan is removed from `docs/` (git history preserves it; zero live-tree inbound references), emptying the `docs/superpowers/` subtree. The consumer-less `src.claude/memory/` surface is deleted together with the dead `OPTIONAL_DIRS` installer mechanism (both Claude installers, plus the empty declaration in the Codex installer) and its four doc mentions; existing installs keep their local `memory/` untouched — nothing ever deletes it in targets. The passive-polling Stop hook drops a provably unreachable branch in all three byte-identical copies (behavior-neutral; the parity test still gates the triple). **Who this affects:** doc readers get the shipped role-skill model everywhere; manifest users get sync anchors that survive rewrites; fresh installs shed a dead copy step with no change to existing targets.

- **Cohesion-polarity closure (audit Wave F): the hook-placement law now matches hook reality, model-initiated inline role-skill adoption is lawful when announced and bounded, the four dual wrappers fail closed, and every layering-law ID cited in an installed role file resolves in-file.** The source-hygiene placement law is amended to a gate-semantics split (decision record `2026-07-11-hook-placement-gate-semantics`): warn-only audit hooks live in the typed `agents/hooks/` dirs with byte-identical canon copies; blocking-enforcement and SessionStart context hooks live in the co-located `agents/scripts/` dirs; and a provider-specialized script with no canon copy must be declared, with justification, in the new `PACK_ONLY_SCRIPTS` allowlist enforced by a scripts/ set-equality test in `tests/test_universal_hook_surfaces.py` — closing the gap that let `agents-mode-reminder.*` ship in both packs invisible to the parity gate; both pack entrypoints now state the honest placement-convention rationale instead of an import-necessity claim disproven in-tree. Inline adoption of the four dual role-skills (`product-manager`, `analyst`, `architect`, `planner`) now has exactly two triggers — operator-explicit invocation, or model-initiated adoption for the ONE bounded decision the quick-fix/fast lane already makes inline, announced in-chat before executing and scoped to that one decision — stated consistently across `CLAUDE.md` and all eight wrapper/skill surfaces; unannounced adoption stays forbidden and inline adoption still satisfies no independent gate. Each dual wrapper now fails closed (`BLOCKED:skill-unavailable`) when its Skill load fails, instead of executing from the wrapper's summary. 36 role files across both production packs now label their layering-law citations with the law ID (the `security-engineer` `**... (C1):**` pattern), four roles gained the missing one-line labeled definition, and both pack validators gained a `[Layering-law ID resolution]` check failing any role file that cites a law ID with no same-file labeled definition (common skills exempt — `mathtype-book-page` uses A1/A4 as paper sizes); the five installed files citing `docs/lessons|decisions|dependencies.md` now mark those as maintainer references not installed at runtime. **Who this affects:** maintainers adding hooks get one decidable placement law with a gate behind it; operators get dual role-skills whose inline/dispatch behavior matches the documented contract; dispatched agents can bind every law ID their role file tells them to name in findings.

- **Guard-teeth closure (audit Wave D): five guard surfaces that could stay green while the invariant they protect was gutted now have real teeth.** (1) Common-skill BODY parity is pinned at the validator level: both pack validators (`validate-skill-pack.sh`, Claude and Codex) carry a body-only normalized-SHA-256 pin for each of the six common skills — the frontmatter stays provider-tailored by design; the body after it is the cross-pack invariant — plus a completeness check that every spine-declared common skill has a pin. A standalone provider branch runs only its own pack validator (the monorepo test suite never ships there), so the exact body-drift class reconciled in Wave A could previously ship silently on a branch; negative-probed: one flipped body character fails the pack validator. (2) The stale-relation-residue audit hook gained its missing behavioral suite (`tests/test_stale_relation_residue_hook.py`, 42 tests driving both pack copies as subprocesses): the documented residue-phrase matrix, the exempt provenance targets (task-memory registry, changelog/release/history stems, archival trees, scratch, git internals — with stem/segment near-misses locked as NOT exempt), the adds-only guarantee (a removed or merely preserved phrase never warns; an edit's old text is never scanned), and fail-open on a malformed envelope; the hook's production behavior is unchanged. (3) The Codex validator's assembled-AGENTS.md section check now pins the same four governance headings the Claude mirror pins, as anchored heading matches — the previous case-insensitive substring check stayed green even with the publication-safety section dropped entirely. (4) The lead-invariant build gate closes two false-pass holes: the split-owner duality is caught with and/both/vs joiners (not only the slash and "or" forms), and the allow-list is now category-scoped per (pattern-category, allow-list-group) — a generic label word ("legacy", "renamed", "refuses"...) may excuse a labeled duality/field mention on its line but can never neutralize a dispatch-of-lead hit; the self-verifying teeth lists lock every new WRONG form, and the scan now also covers the shipped `src.gemini`/`src.qwen` trees (zero hits at pin time). (5) The agent-run ledger's `executionRole` contract collapses to ONE main-conversation identity (`main`): the schema enum drops the second owner-shaped value, old ledger lines carrying it still READ via a documented legacy mapping (and roll up into the `main` audit bucket instead of splitting one owner across two), a NEW append with the legacy value is rejected with a pointer to `main`, and the ledger contract check asserts the schema models exactly one main-conversation identity. **Who this affects:** operators on standalone provider branches get cross-pack body-parity enforcement from the branch's own validator; ledger audits can no longer split one orchestrator across two buckets; the guard suite now proves what its green actually claims.

- **Model-currency reconciliation across the Claude line (audit Wave C): explicit Claude launch flags, a Claude-side enum drift guard that survives family renames, `fable-xhigh` in the Claude execution vocabulary, and honest currency annotations on the routing evidence.** The Claude prompt wrapper (`invoke-claude-prompt.sh` / `.ps1`) and the consultant's primary-Claude launch snippet now pass explicit `--model opus --effort xhigh` by default instead of riding whatever ambient model the operator's Claude config selects — the same explicit-launch-flags (A12) fix the Codex wrapper received on 2026-07-07, closing a silent consultant xhigh-floor breach on the Claude line; an explicit `--` flag block still overrides everything, including the model. The enum-copy drift guard in `scripts/validate-agents-mode-contract.py` no longer hardcodes one model-family namespace: the token shape is derived from the schema's allowed values at runtime (so a future family rename re-derives the scan instead of silently no-opping), and a new `validate_claude_profile_enum` mirrors the Codex guard for `externalClaudeProfile` across the same discovered doc/config trees, with failure messages naming the offending file and line — negative-probed on two live copies plus a simulated family-jump. Under that guard, the Claude execution vocabulary gained `fable-xhigh` (`--model fable --effort xhigh`), the current flagship-family best-effort profile, propagated to every enum copy and mapping table; the `fable` flagship alias as of 2026-07 is recorded from the installed model list — the CLI lists models but does not rank them, so this is a record-and-flag currency entry, not a verified capability ranking. `opus-xhigh` remains the shipped default, presets are unchanged, and the review-loop deep-verdict lane now names the flagship alias instead of asserting a literal `opus` capability tier. Finally, the 2026-05-01 routing-evidence note and both external-dispatch contracts carry an explicit currency annotation — lane priorities are carried over from the retired benchmark generation pending re-benchmark — plus a new standing rule: a model-family migration in `externalCodexProfile` / `externalClaudeProfile` invalidates the routing-evidence `PASS` until the lanes are re-benchmarked or explicitly re-affirmed on the current families. The re-benchmark itself is tracked as separate follow-up work, not part of this change.

- **The publication-safety scanner no longer fails its own staged scan (gate self-block), and the self-exemption stays keyed to the scanner's own filename.** With the scanner copies themselves staged, the gate exited 1 on the scanner's OWN pre-existing intentional content: the macOS temp-dir entries in its pattern catalog, the body of its previous 5-exact-line self-exemption, a marker-bearing comment, and two fallback path regexes flagged by the embedded machine-local-path filter — so any commit shipping the scanner would block itself. The self-exemption now recognizes the scanner's intentional content structurally instead of by exact line: a line that is ENTIRELY one single-quoted pattern-catalog literal, or a full-line comment, is exempt; the embedded Python machine-local-path filter applies the same scanner-file awareness. The exemption remains keyed to the scanner's own filename (`check-publication-safety.sh`), so the same content staged under any other filename — or a catalog line carrying a trailing payload inside the scanner — still blocks; probe-confirmed: with the full wave staged the scanner passes on itself while a planted lowercase credential file still blocks. Regression pins added to `tests/test_publication_safety_scanner.py`: BLOCK rows for a synthetic `sk-ant-…` token, an `ANTHROPIC_…KEY` assignment, and every casing of the staged credential filename; a PASS row for a prose prefix mention; and a self-scan pin asserting the real scanner source passes under its own name and blocks under any other name.

### Changed

- **`bug-hunting` common-skill body reconciled across all four packs — the P4 second-cross-break stop and the P3 N-symptoms/N-hypotheses rules now ship everywhere.** The four pack copies of `skills/bug-hunting/SKILL.md` had drifted: the **second-cross-break stop** (if a second fix in the same session breaks a previously working neighbor, STOP all edits and run a read-only multi-angle structural diagnosis — owning invariant and call/data flow, sibling modes/surfaces, timing/lifecycle/shared-state — before any further edit) existed only in the Codex copy, and the **N-symptoms rule** (keep N independent root hypotheses for N reported failing cases; collapse to one common root only when one observed mechanism explains every case — shared timing, location, or correlation is not proof) was missing from the Gemini/Qwen copies. Both rules are now present in all four packs (Claude gained P4; Gemini/Qwen gained P4+P3; Codex already had both), restoring the byte-level cross-pack parity the common-skill contract expects.

- **Repo-local full-suite gate for skill/hook commits (`CLAUDE.md`).** Any commit touching `src.*/skills/` or the hook/safety scripts (`scripts/universal-hooks/`, the Claude `agents/scripts/` + `agents/hooks/` dirs, the Codex `lead/scripts/` + `lead/hooks/` dirs) must now run the FULL `python -m pytest tests/` suite to completion before commit — a timed-out or partial run is UNVERIFIED, not clean, because cross-pack parity and canon-mirror drift are only caught by the full suite. `INSTALL.md` also documents the new project-install credential-`.gitignore` guarantee and the scanner's credential-content patterns.

## 2026-07-10

### Added

- **Roles-as-skills curated subset: four Claude roles (`product-manager`, `analyst`, `architect`, `planner`) join `lead` as inline-adoptable role-skills, additive and Claude-only.** Previously only `lead` could be adopted inline (via `/lead`) without an Agent-tool dispatch; every other role required a fresh-context subagent even for a light, in-session decision the main conversation was already making informally (a quick-fix seam call, a trivial factual read, an improvised fast-lane plan). Now `product-manager`, `analyst`, `architect`, and `planner` are **dual** role-skills: the canonical contract for each moved to `.claude/skills/<role>/SKILL.md` (`/`-invocable, preserves the current conversation's accumulated context) and `.claude/agents/<role>.md` became a thin fresh-context delegate wrapper (the same shape already shipped for `windows-gui-manual-testing`) whose required first step loads the same skill — so `subagent_type: product-manager|analyst|architect|planner` stays a fully valid, unchanged dispatch target. **The curated criterion (decidable, structurally enforced):** a role qualifies only if none of its value depends on separation from the invoking conversation, AND governance already names a recurring moment where the main conversation legitimately does that role's work inline. `product-manager` carries an explicit separation caveat baked into its skill and into `CLAUDE.md`: inline adoption is for quick intake/scope framing only — a formal cross-initiative roadmap decision, or admitting work that will GATE other work, must still route to the `product-manager` SUBAGENT so admission authority stays separated from the conversation that will go on to execute the work. **Explicitly excluded, and why:** the consultant (its value is an independent opinion from a different model — inline adoption defeats that, and `/agents-second-opinion` already owns the intent); every formal reviewer and QA role (a gate's authority IS its independence from the authoring conversation — an inline "review" is a self-review, not a gate); every implementer and external adapter (isolated execution of an approved phase is the value, not economy); the remaining constraint-owner and product-analyst roles (no governance-named recurring inline moment). **Structural enforcement:** `validate-skill-pack.sh` gained a `[Curated role-skill registry]` block — a POSITIVE check that each of the 5 curated roles has both surfaces in the expected shape (skill file present; the 4 duals' agent wrapper pins to its skill via the `Skill` tool and is not accidentally a fail-closed stub), and a NEGATIVE ALLOWLIST that FAILs the build if any other role-index role ever grows a `skills/<role>/SKILL.md` — making the curation criterion structural, not just documented, and giving `lead`'s existing menu-budget concern a decidable gate. `CLAUDE.md`'s "every specialist invocation MUST use the Agent tool" rule gained a narrow, explicit exception naming exactly these five roles, only on explicit `/`-invoke, never claiming isolation or independence. **Preserved:** the shared 33-role taxonomy and spine are untouched (zero-diff); team-template JSON routing is unchanged; every reviewer/QA/consultant/implementer/external-adapter role stays Agent-tool-only; Codex is already skills-only for every role (no change); Gemini/Qwen remain deferred per the admitted scope. The architect role's contract body — including the P8 writer-owner/settled-event Change-Surface Contract rule — moved with it to `skills/architect/SKILL.md`; the cross-pack `arch-layering-slices` drift gate and the orchestration-discipline contract test were updated to point at the new location instead of the old `agents/architect.md`, matching the shape Codex already used. Decision to be filed at synthesis: `2026-07-10-roles-as-skills-curated`.

- **New design-generation panel technique (`design-panel`) — independent multi-lane design generation with mandatory synthesis, shipped on the Claude and Codex production lines only (Gemini/Qwen demo mirror deferred).** design-panel is the generation-side analog of the existing review-loop: where review-loop converges multiple angles on ONE already-written artifact across autonomous rounds, design-panel dispatches N (default 2) independently-framed design lanes on ONE pinned problem BEFORE a single design exists, then requires one mandatory synthesis to produce the sole planner-eligible `design.md` — a candidate lane can never itself become the accepted design (`DP4`), and only the synthesis step may carry the design-stage `PASS` (`DP6`). Two admitted triggers: a high-surface-count mechanical sweep, or an open architecture choice; it is a deliberate, heavier surface, not the default `/agents-design` route. **Shipped:** a provider-neutral trunk (`shared/references/design-panel-methodology.md` + `ru/` mirror) defining the stable `DP1`-`DP8` invariant IDs; a self-contained Claude binding (`agents/contracts/design-panel.md` + thin `/agents-design-panel` command, narrow explicit triggers only); a self-contained Codex binding (`skills/design-panel/SKILL.md` + `agents/openai.yaml`, registered in `UTILITY_SKILLS` under the unchanged metadata cap); a new "Design-panel and review-loop selection" block in both `operating-model.md` files (neither file referenced review-loop before this change — verified zero-hit — so this introduces both pointers together); one-line reverse "see also" cross-links in `review-loop.md`/`agents-review-loop.md`; and dispatch-index/help/README/INSTALL/`docs/provider-runtime-layouts.md`/`cross-pack-reconciliation.md` updates naming the new binding paths. **Two independent skip-guards, of different strength, defend against a skipped synthesis:** (1) canonical-filename reservation — the STRUCTURAL guard: candidate lanes write `design-<lane>.md`, only synthesis writes `design.md`, and the existing plan-stage gate already requires `design.md`, so a skipped synthesis leaves no artifact the pipeline can consume; (2) a RECOMMENDED, AUDITABLE ledger convention reusing the existing `agent-runs.jsonl` — a candidate run should record `gate: RETURN(lead)`, never design-stage `PASS`. A smoke probe confirmed `scripts/validate-work-item-state.py` does not mechanically reject a candidate marked `PASS` nor a `RETURN(lead)` run without a cited artifact, so guard (2) is an auditable convention for review to catch, not a validator-enforced gate — the actual mechanical skip-prevention is guard (1) plus the `INPUT_ONLY` candidate label. **Explicitly not shipped:** no `design-panel-state` schema, no new state-validator script, and no `shared/AGENTS.shared.md` spine edit (zero characters, verified `git diff --exit-code`) — this is a conditional orchestration technique outside the always-loaded governance layer. The pack validators check only mechanical structure (file presence, `DP1`-`DP8`/`INPUT_ONLY`/`RETURN(lead)` marker presence); whether two framings are substantively independent and whether the synthesis found every unique contribution stay review territory.

### Changed

- **Lead is a main-conversation ROLE, never a spawned subagent — routing, recovery, and role contracts corrected across all packs; on the Claude line the Lead contract now ships as the `/lead` skill with `agents/lead.md` as a fail-closed dispatch stub.** Previously the claude/codex governance surfaces taught `requiresLead: true → invoke $lead via the Agent tool`, which made the orchestrating conversation spawn a throwaway lead subagent — losing session context and demoting the orchestrator to a relay. Now every surface (spine, routing docs, contracts, commands, the live-injected `agents-mode-reminder` directives, the shared operating-model reference and its ru mirror) states one invariant: the MAIN CONVERSATION holds the Lead role, dispatches only leaf specialist subagents (Claude) / activates role skills (Codex), and owns `work-items/` recovery and integration for every chain; `requiresLead` now means orchestration WEIGHT (light vs full lead pipeline), not a different owner. The recovery-file field `orchestrator: main | lead` is superseded by `orchestration: light | full-lead` (legacy values read main→light, lead→full-lead). Closing a work-item is now explicitly split: the deciding role (main conversation as Lead) owns the close decision and `closure.md` content; `$knowledge-archivist` owns the index-sync/archive-move/reconciliation mechanics after every state change (routine single-item closes may be applied inline under that contract). The Lead role also becomes skill-aware: at each routing point it considers applicable process/verification skills (spine `## Common skills` owner; superpowers composition per CLAUDE.md — examples only, nothing hard-required). **Who this affects:** operators of both packs get correct orchestration on the next install; `/lead` becomes explicitly invocable on Claude to adopt the Lead role in-session under `delegationMode: manual/auto`. **Preserved:** team-template JSON is byte-unchanged (`requiresLead`/`chain` field names intact); leaf-specialist dispatch stays mandatory; `$external-worker`/`$external-reviewer` keep direct external launch; a stale `subagent_type: lead` dispatch now fails CLOSED (refuses and points to `/lead`) instead of silently orchestrating. Decision: `2026-07-10-lead-is-mainconv-role` (proposed).

- **Codex model migration: `gpt-5.5`/`gpt-5.3` family retired in favor of `gpt-5.6-sol`/`gpt-5.6-luna`, and the Codex prompt wrapper now launches the model explicitly instead of leaving it ambient.** `externalCodexProfile` now resolves to three concrete profiles plus `default` (unchanged — still inherits `externalModelMode`): `gpt-5.6-sol-xhigh` (shipped default, symmetric to Claude's `opus-xhigh`), `gpt-5.6-sol-max` (higher-complexity/hard lanes), and `gpt-5.6-luna` (bounded fast/volume overflow tier, replacing both the former `gpt-5.5-fast` and `gpt-5.3-codex-spark` values). No `gpt-5.6-sol-ultra` profile ships — `ultra` spawns subagents itself and must never be assigned to a subagent lane. Separately, `src.claude/agents/scripts/invoke-codex-prompt.{sh,ps1}`'s no-explicit-flags default path previously supplied only `-c model_reasoning_effort=xhigh`, leaving the MODEL itself ambient (whatever the caller's `codex` config resolved); it now defaults to `--model gpt-5.6-sol -c model_reasoning_effort=xhigh` (the shipped `gpt-5.6-sol-xhigh` profile) so every default-path launch carries an explicit model per the `A12` explicit-launch-flags rule below. A caller that already passes an explicit `--` flag block is unaffected. **Who this affects:** operators relying on the shipped default profile or the wrapper's no-flags path get the new model family automatically on next use; any repo-local or pack-local reference still naming `gpt-5.5`/`gpt-5.3` tokens as a live enum value should be updated to match. **Preserved:** `externalCodexProfile: default` (inherit `externalModelMode`) is unchanged and remains the intentional "inherit shared model policy" value in the balanced presets.

- **Closed three orchestration-discipline gaps mirrored across both packs: a declared-marker exception for the worktree-isolation audit hook, a fail-closed polarity fix for a dead review-loop lane, and mandatory explicit model/effort flags on every provider-backed launch.** (1) The `check-no-trash-in-repo` stray-artifact AUDIT hook previously warned on every `git worktree add`. It now also recognizes the new parallel-isolation protocol's discriminator: a command containing exactly ONE `git worktree add` whose text ends with the literal `# orchestrarium:requested-isolation-worktree` marker is a declared, protocol-requested isolation worktree and is not warned; a missing, near-match, quoted, reused, or batch-added marker still warns, so the guard stays fail-closed to warning by default. This pairs with a new operating-model rule (both `operating-model.md` files): a parallel lane that may mutate the working tree or invoke Git MUST run in its own isolated worktree — disjoint file lists alone no longer count as isolation — declared with the marker only after naming the lane and isolation reason in prose, with the main conversation owning integration and cleanup. (2) The spine and both review-loop contracts (Claude `agents/contracts/review-loop.md`, Codex `skills/review-loop/SKILL.md`) close a silent fail-open gap: an expected verdict or scout lane that errors, dies, or hits a time/token/usage limit is now explicitly UNVERIFIED rather than risking a silence-read-as-clean result. The spine gains "An errored/died/limit-hit lane is UNVERIFIED... an aggregation/gating script is production code: a missing/null sub-verdict counts NOT-clean (fail-closed)"; the review-loop contracts add matching hardening invariants 7 (`Failed lane is unverified` — record the failed attempt, re-dispatch it, never infer a clean result from silence) and 8 (`Fail-closed aggregation` — a missing/null sub-verdict or findings payload is NOT-clean; the gate stays `REVISE` until every lane has substantive output and every failure is reconciled). The `review-loop-state` ledger schema gained a per-angle `attempt_id` and a `lane_failures` list so a reconciled failure is auditable in the trace. **The ledger's structural validator (`scripts/validate-review-loop-state.{py,sh,ps1}`) is unaffected by this change and stays repo/dev-only** — it is still NOT part of any installer's runtime-helper set, per the standing operator directive recorded above (the `2026-06-03` entry **"Kept the review-loop ledger validator repo/dev-only — not installed to the global."**) that the global install carries no repo-specific dev tooling; the runtime enforcement for a shipped loop is invariants 7-8 above, not an installed validator run. (3) External-dispatch (`external-dispatch.md`, both packs) tightens `externalModelMode: runtime-default`: it must now be resolved to the installed runtime's observed supported model/effort and passed as explicit launch flags before launch instead of silently riding an ambient provider config default. The new universal rule applies to every provider-backed run: it "MUST carry the resolved model/profile and effort as explicit launch flags in that invocation, even when they equal configured defaults; never rely on provider config defaults," reporting the route unavailable when `runtime-default` cannot be made explicit, with the exact flags recorded in the execution artifact. Why it matters: each of the three closes a silent-failure surface (unrequested worktree noise, a dead review-loop lane counted as clean, and an ambient config default masquerading as a verified launch) with a mirrored, mechanically-pinned normative sentence in both the Claude and Codex packs.

## 2026-07-09

### Changed

- **Shipped `default` preset now sets `delegationMode: auto` (was `manual`), so a fresh install applies delegation-by-routing-judgment without an `/agents-init-project` run.** Before this, the `default` preset — the safe-init baseline seeded on first install — set `delegationMode: manual`, so the new `agents-mode-reminder` SessionStart hook stayed SILENT on a clean install and no delegation posture reached the model until an operator opted in.
