Flipping the shipped default to `auto` (deliberately NOT `force`, which is a standing instruction too aggressive to impose on a third-party machine without opt-in) means the hook now surfaces the AUTO delegation directive automatically on a default install: routing non-trivial tasks to `$lead`/the matching specialist becomes the default, chosen by routing judgment. **Who this affects:** new installs pick up `auto` immediately; any existing config that OMITS `delegationMode` acquires `auto` on the next reinstall/normalize (normalization fills only missing canonical keys — it never overwrites a key already present). **Preserved:** every explicit `delegationMode` value is kept verbatim by normalize, so an operator's `force` (or an explicit `manual`) survives untouched; the other five presets (`absolute-balance`, `external-aggressive`, `correctness-first`, `power-mode`, `max-speed`) and all other scalar keys are unchanged; the hook's `force`/`auto` directive text is unchanged; and the no-file/unresolved state stays fail-safe SILENT (the hook's no-file fallback now resolves to a distinct `unresolved` sentinel instead of reusing the `manual` enum, so the code no longer implies the default is `manual`). **This supersedes the same-day "SILENT on `manual` (the built-in default)" framing** of the `agents-mode-reminder` entry below: the built-in/shipped default is now `auto`, and the hook is silent on the explicit `manual` value and on the no-file/unresolved state — not because `manual` is the default. Landed across the three shared owner files (`agents-mode.defaults.yaml`, `agents-mode.schema.json`, `agents-mode.presets.json`), the generated init/reference preset-expansion tables and canonical-shape comments, the hand-maintained first-write surfaces in both packs, the four `agents-mode-reminder.{sh,ps1}` hook scripts, and the contract validator plus its tests.

### Added

- **New `agents-mode-reminder` SessionStart hook makes an active `delegationMode` actually reach the model (both packs; eighth auto-installed hook entry).** `delegationMode: force` / `auto` in `.agents-mode.yaml` is Orchestrator-pack governance the host (Claude Code / Codex CLI) never parses on its own — so before this hook, a `force` config silently did nothing: the main conversation never saw the posture, never routed non-trivial work to `$lead`/specialists, and stopped maintaining `work-items/` recovery state. The new hook (`agents-mode-reminder.{sh,ps1}`, mirrored per pack, registered on `SessionStart` with no matcher so it re-fires on startup/resume/clear/compaction alongside `mcp-usage-reminder`) does a self-contained first-match walk of the documented read-order, and **only** when the effective mode is `force` or `auto` injects an imperative directive to classify-and-route at the first decision point and keep work-items state; it is **SILENT on `manual`** (the built-in default) so the block's presence is itself the signal and never becomes always-on wallpaper. Generic and fail-open (any error emits nothing, exits 0, never blocks a session). **Read-order per pack:** Claude walks `./.claude/…` → `~/.claude/…` → `~/.agents-mode.yaml`; Codex walks `./.agents/…` → `~/.codex/…` → `~/.agents-mode.yaml`. **Correctness hardening (external Codex review, all confirmed and fixed before ship):** the top-level `^delegationMode:` key is matched case-sensitively (`grep`/`-cmatch`) so `DelegationMode:` and nested profile keys never match; a comment is stripped only when whitespace precedes `#` (so a literal value like `force#x` stays unrecognized → silent); only the ends are trimmed (so `fo rce` stays split → silent, never a false FORCE); the first file whose key LINE is present OWNS the decision even if its value is empty/unrecognized (a lower layer's `force` can no longer override a higher layer that cleared or set it); the PowerShell path uses `$homeDir` (not the read-only automatic `$HOME`) so `$env:USERPROFILE` is honored. The `.sh` and `.ps1` paths were verified to emit identical verdicts across the full edge matrix. **Affected workflow:** operators who set `delegationMode: force`/`auto` globally or per-project now get the delegation posture applied automatically every session and after every compaction, without needing `/agents-init-project` or a manual reminder. Auto-installed by all four installers (`install-claude.{sh,ps1}`, `install-codex.{sh,ps1}`); remove independently with `--hook-event SessionStart --script-marker agents-mode-reminder … --remove` (Codex requires the usual manual trust step in the `codex` TUI before it fires).

## 2026-07-07

### Changed

- **The no-fix-layering rule's defense-in-depth boundary test sharpened from transport-based to TRUST-based.** The test for when a duplicate check is legitimate (not layering) previously keyed on transport — "producer and consumer are different processes OR communicate through a persisted artifact". It now keys on TRUST: defense-in-depth is legitimate ONLY when the value could actually cross the boundary untrusted or corrupted (untrusted user input, a third-party system, or a persisted/generated artifact a different producer or hand-edit could have written), with agreed thresholds and a commit justification — a mere process or network hop between two of the author's own components is NOT such a boundary. Adds the re-apply/re-check distinction: re-applying the owner's result (calling/using/enforcing its output) is always fine (consumption, not duplication), while re-checking the result (re-verifying it because you distrust it) is layering unless it crossed an untrusted/corruptible boundary. **Effect:** exactly one reviewer verdict class flips — a downstream layer that re-checks or re-derives a trusted internal producer's already-computed output is now decisively LAYERING, where the old transport test ambiguously permitted it as "different processes"; no other verdict changes. Landed in `shared/references/spine/no-fix-layering-one-correct-logic.md` (the decidable boundary-test section) + the `JUSTIFIED-DEPTH` verdict definition in `$architecture-reviewer` across all four packs, and the spine clause's own boundary phrase was re-keyed transport→trust (cap-safe); the fuller nuance stays in the reference, not the spine. The proposal's wider "process/storage/network" boundary list was deliberately dropped (mixing transport items back in would re-open the hole). Sourced from an operator proposal, narrowed by a Fable-lens skeptic evaluation to the one genuinely-new decidable kernel; decision `2026-07-07-defense-in-depth-trust-axis-refinement`.

### Added

`--dry-run`-safe, covered by `tests/test_install_claude_namespace_reclaim.py`, documented in INSTALL.md, decision `2026-07-07-claude-install-surface-ownership`. **(3) Standalone docs/README is GENERATED, not frozen:** `extract-provider-branch.py` rebuilds each branch's `docs/README.md` from the monorepo copy with the "Current docs in this branch" list regenerated from the docs actually shipped (excluded subtrees like `docs/routing/` are dropped, no dead links) — replacing a frozen copy that had drifted to naming 2 docs while the branch shipped 8. **(4) universal-hooks canon completed + self-maintaining:** `check-stale-relation-residue.{py,sh,ps1}` (shipped in the packs since bea9c7f but never added to the pack-neutral canon) is now in `scripts/universal-hooks/hooks/`; the canon dir is the single owner — `test_universal_hook_surfaces.py`, `validate-agents-mode-installers.py`, and all four gemini/qwen installers now DERIVE the hook list by globbing the canon (never a hardcoded tuple), and a new set-equality test flags any pack hook missing from the canon (the exact gap that hid it). **(5) Local-only .gitignore tiers:** `shared/local-only-tiers.txt` is the owner; - **New standing governance rule "no fix layering — one correct logic", placed across five existing owners (operator-mandated).** Fixes must never pile as patches: every invariant/decision logic has ONE owner, and a fix corrects the owner's logic — never adds a neighboring check because the first one "wasn't trusted" or wasn't found. Placement (per an `$architect` design memo): **(1) spine** — the `No logic duplication` clause is sharpened into `No logic duplication / no fix layering`, replacing its vague exception sentence with the decidable form (re-checking one invariant at several heights because the first check was not trusted is layering, not defense; legitimate defense-in-depth needs a process/artifact boundary, agreed thresholds, a commit justification); the zero-sum donor is a compaction of `State-synchronization ownership` that sheds only illustrative enumerations — all nine operative tests and the pinned literal tokens are preserved (external Codex substance-loss review: PASS), spine 39,888 ≤ 39,900. **(2) Full reference** (new, maintainer-side): `shared/references/spine/no-fix-layering-one-correct-logic.md` — the five red flags (N guards at M heights; producer+consumer duplicate validation without a process boundary; a fix papering over a fix; an if-else pile begging for a table; an interim stub without a tracked root-cause item), the decidable boundary test, the audit methodology, and a generalized canonical case. **(3) Anti-layering audit lane**: `$architecture-reviewer` gains a standing `## Anti-layering audit (multi-fix batch)` section (all four packs; placed outside the slice-validated block) — for any batch with 2+ defect fixes, group changes by defect class and verify ONE owner per class; per-class verdicts `CLEAN-SINGLE-OWNER` / `JUSTIFIED-DEPTH` / `PILED`, where PILED maps to `REVISE` and blocks push; the lane runs on an engine distinct from the batch's author via the normal routing surface (no hardcoded model name ships). Trigger bullets added to the claude `agents-review` command and codex `review-changes` skill. **(4) Law D1 amended** (single owner of failure-idiom policy): a third legitimate layer idiom — an in-band poison value (e.g. NaN-poison) ONLY where no status channel exists — plus the idiom-uniformity probe (two failure idioms for one failure class within one layer is a finding); EN+RU references and both D1 slice variants refreshed across claude+codex, slice stamp regenerated. **(5) Periodic scan**: the `Refactor debt scan` matrix row (claude+codex EN+RU) now includes the repo-wide anti-layering pass. The symptom-guard discipline is deliberately NOT restated — the spine no-kostyl rule already owns it. Decisions filed: `2026-07-07-no-fix-layering-rule-placement`, `2026-07-07-d1-failure-idiom-uniformity-amendment` (proposed). **Why it matters:** incremental fix rounds that each add "one more special case" to the same loop are now a named, gate-blocking defect class with a decidable boundary between layering and legitimate defense-in-depth.
- **vak-dissertation-review verification discipline extended (installed-copy drift backported; all four packs byte-identical).** The operator's live review sessions advanced the installed copy of `references/verification-discipline.md` well past the repo source; the delta is now the repo canon: §3 gains the mirror of the don't-delete trap (a note that QUOTES the reviewed work's error must preserve the wrong form verbatim — "fixing" the quote erases the very defect being reported and reads as self-contradiction to a consistency scan); §8 gains two tooling rules (check existing project notes before deep-debugging a tool; prefer the project's pinned known-good binary over a system package build); new **§10** — ВАК publication counting reads a journal's Перечень status at each article's PUBLICATION DATE (per-article-by-year audit, transitional clauses of the governing regulation located before applying current thresholds, primary sources over aggregators, proof chain assembled in the document); new **§11** — multi-document review packages need cross-document consistency (drift = two sheets stating different things about the same fact, NOT one sheet's legitimate silence; two-phase cross-check: verbatim extraction then adversarial confirm/reject with default REJECT); new **§12** — collect cited sources into one tree with a manifest (verify each file IS the claimed edition; track collected vs reference-only honestly; primary artifact over secondary description for load-bearing facts).

## 2026-06-19

### Added

- **The generalized architecture-layering reference gained a runtime/operational law group D (D1–D5), a capstone abstraction-level meta-law M, and a no-residue law C6 — extending the design methodology every architect, reviewer, and implementer applies from three axes (structure / performance / stability) to four (adding runtime/operational behavior).** Previously `shared/references/architecture-layering-hygiene.md` (and its RU mirror) stated decidable, owner-and-probe-bearing laws only for structure (A1–A7), performance (B1–B3), and stability/reproducibility (C1–C5). The new content, generalized from a numerical-solver layer-library rule set plus an operator no-residue rule and stripped of domain specifics, closes the runtime gap in the same form (decidable test + named owner + enforcement probe + defaults with named exceptions): **D1** failure is a typed RETURNED value and only the composition root terminates the process (a leaf that calls `exit`/`abort`/`panic` is unembeddable); **D2** observability flows through one support-owned injected diagnostic port with a const-registry of versioned event IDs and a zero-residue disabled path on measured loops; **D3** every result/golden/validation/release run emits a publication-safe run manifest (toolchain, pinned deps, platform identity, determinism/FP mode, seed, parallel config, input hashes, allowlist-built config snapshot) — broader than output equivalence and a HARD packaging-fail when missing/divergent; **D4** resource lifetime and mutable process-global state are composition-root-owned with cleanup on every exit path including cancel/timeout; **D5** parallel regions classify mutable state per datum (immutable / worker-owned / exactly-associative-integer-or-bitwise atomic-summary / merge-owner) and merge in the C1-canonical order, with no serializing lock on a measured hot loop. The capstone **M** ("abstract by default, concretize only where genuinely required") anchors the structural laws A1–C5 — an owner edit forced by a new instance is the violation, and a one-instance indirection layer is the equal-and-opposite over-abstraction failure. New law **C6** ("a superseding change leaves only the correct current state; stale-relation residue is erased") lives in group C as the change-hygiene complement to C5: C5 tracks blessed current debt, C6 erases superseded residue (stale aliases, `was-X` comments, dead pointers, leaked historical identifiers), keeping only LIVE relations via a review-bound stale-vs-live discriminator. The existing A1–A7 / B1–B3 / C1–C5 statements are unchanged. **Why it matters:** reviewers and designers now have a decidable, probe-bearing checklist for failure transparency, observability contracts, reproducibility/provenance, resource ownership, parallel-data determinism, abstraction level, and post-change residue — risks that were previously governed only loosely by the always-on spine. The change was accepted by a nine-angle commission (opus-max architect + Sonnet + reliability + performance + security + computational-scientist + architecture-reviewer + knowledge-archivist, plus an external Codex gpt-5.5 faithful-application review). The separately-reviewed WARN-mode `check-stale-relation-residue` audit hook for C6 shipped the same day (see the entry below; audit-first, never auto-blocking, because the stale-vs-live discriminator needs human judgment).
- **A new `release` validator group splits the packaging/release implementers (`platform-engineer`, `toolchain-engineer`) out of the generic `impl` group in `scripts/validate-arch-layering-slices.py`.** The release roles own CI/CD and build packaging, so their architecture-layering slice carries the implementer laws PLUS D3 (the run-manifest contract). Splitting them into their own byte-identity group is required: adding D3 to platform/toolchain while they remained in `impl` would break the within-group byte-identity axis the drift gate enforces. **Affected guidance per role:** design (architect) and review (architecture-reviewer) slices now carry M + D1–D5 + C6; reliability-engineer carries D1–D5 + C6; performance-engineer/performance-reviewer carry the D2 compile-elision and D5 lock/merge facets; the eight implementer roles carry M + D1 + D2-structural + D4 + D5 + C6; platform/toolchain additionally carry D3; algorithm-scientist/computational-scientist carry M + D3 + D5; security-engineer carries the D3 publication-safe-snapshot facet; qa-engineer carries D1/D3/D4/D5/C6 as verification facets. The condensed slices stay byte-identical within each group and across the Claude and Codex packs, as the drift gate requires; the reference SHA review stamp was regenerated to record the commission's fidelity re-review.
- **Strengthened the `$architect` contract and folded a universal claim-owner-probe rule into the spine — making the design seam a single-owner contract that downstream roles consume but cannot redefine.** Five operator-accepted governance moves, shipped symmetrically to both the Claude (`src.claude/agents/`) and Codex (`src.codex/skills/`) packs. **(Move 1) Change-Surface Contract:** `$architect`'s design package now carries a named, required `{ intended change surface, approved extension seam(s), protected / must-not-touch surfaces, declared blast radius }` field — the architect OWNS the seam/blast-radius decision; `$planner` consumes it and allocates phases WITHIN it (escalating any phase that cannot fit as a `REVISE`-to-architect, replacing the old "send it back for design review" prose), and the eight implementer roles get a working rule that a forced scenario-specific edit to a stable/shared module is a `REVISE`-to-architect, not an implementer judgment call. The `quick-fix` fast lane is exempt — it carries no design stage and no contract. **(Move 2) Decisions-registry citation as a contract:** `$architect`'s gate now requires every cross-cutting/long-lived decision in the claims section to carry a `work-items/decisions/` id (authored `proposed`), and `$architecture-reviewer` returns a blocking `REVISE` for any such decision asserted without an id; the trigger is narrow (decisions that outlive the work-item or constrain others) so local single-work-item decisions stay inline in `design.md` and the registry does not flood. `$lead`'s Decisions section documents the two-way contract. **(Move 3) Surgical template edits (not architect-everywhere):** `performance-sensitive.json` makes `architect` a CONDITIONAL role (`whenNeeded: "the budget fix changes a seam, contract, or dependency direction"`; a within-seam tune needs no architect — `requiredRoles`/`minRoles` adjusted from 6→5), and `quick-fix.json` notes formalize the decidable re-classification trigger ("a NEW seam, a shared/core module touch, or a contract change pulls in the architect") while leaving `requiredRoles`/`minRoles` unchanged so the fast lane stays fast. **(Move 4) Typed claims schema:** each architect claim is now a fixed `{ guarantee, single-owner, enforcement-probe }` triple, and `$architecture-reviewer` maps it 1:1 — finding N checks exactly claim N's owner and probe. **(Q2) Universal claim rule:** the spine `Evidence-citation discipline` line gained a trailing clause — every load-bearing claim in an authored artifact names its owner + a falsifying probe (`file:line`/command/test/gate), else `ASSUMPTION (UNVERIFIED)` — folded in (not distributed per-role, which would over-distribute and need a second byte-identity validator) by tightening the same line's non-teeth prose so the always-loaded spine stays under its 39,900-codepoint cap with all pinned protection tokens intact. **Why it matters:** the seam/blast-radius decision now has exactly one owner and thin consumers, a cross-cutting decision can no longer slip through review without a durable registry id, performance and quick-fix work pull in design authority only when the change actually crosses a seam, and every authored claim everywhere is falsifiable by a named probe. Move 5 (a warn-only stale-proposed-decision Stop hook) is a separately-reviewed follow-on chunk.
- **WARN-mode `check-stale-relation-residue` PreToolUse audit hook shipped (both production packs) — the structural backstop for law C6.** The hook (`check-stale-relation-residue.py` + thin `.sh`/`.ps1` wrappers in the typed `hooks/` dir of each pack) warns when an `Edit`/`Write`/`apply_patch` ADDS a stale-relation residue phrase — the fixed C6 vocabulary that almost always asserts an OBSOLETE relationship a completed rename/merge/deprecation/move/fix should have erased (`deprecated alias`, `former alias`/`former name`/`former internal identifier`, `now-retired … kept as a historical example`, `(was X)`/`(formerly X)`/`(previously X)`/`(renamed from X)` parentheticals, `misregistered as`, arrow+`alias`, `…is wrong … correct is Y`) — into a LIVE-tree file. Diff-aware: for unified-diff/apply_patch payloads it scans ONLY added lines, so REMOVING residue (exactly what C6 wants) never warns. Exempt provenance surfaces where recording a superseded relation IS legitimate: `work-items/` registries, changelog/release/history docs (exact filename stems, so a live `history_parser.py` is NOT exempt), `/archive/`+`/legacy/`+`/_archive/` trees, `.scratch/`, `.git/`. WARN-only by design — the stale-vs-live discriminator is review-bound (a real dependency or current comparison uses the same words), so it surfaces candidates and ALWAYS allows; both installers wire it as the third audit entry (default `Edit|Write|NotebookEdit|apply_patch` matcher), removable independently. The same change fixed a silent Windows no-op in `hook_common.read_stdin_utf8` for ALL audit hooks: PowerShell can prepend a UTF-8 BOM when piping stdin, which made `json.loads` reject the envelope; decoding with `utf-8-sig` strips it (applied to both packs' `hook_common.py` copies).

Plain stdout becomes added context on both Claude Code and Codex — verified against the official Codex hooks contract: SessionStart adds stdout as developer context and its source matcher includes `compact`, so a separate PostCompact hook was unnecessary (and PostCompact cannot inject context anyway). **Generic by design:** it names NO specific MCP server (a hardcoded machine-local list would be wrong to ship — it would also trip the pack's own machine-local-path audit), so the agent discovers the actual servers via tool discovery; the reminder also states that dispatched subagents inherit all tools including every connected MCP server and may use any at their discretion. `install-hypothesis-hook.py` gained `SessionStart` as a registerable event (one-line `--hook-event` choices extension; the existing matcher logic already omits a matcher for non-PreToolUse events). ## 2026-06-13

### Added

- **First-class Epics — group multiple work-items under one goal or milestone.** Adds the agile Epic -> Story -> Task hierarchy to Orchestrarium's file-based task memory (epic -> work-item -> phase). An epic is a flat local file `work-items/epics/<date>-<slug>.md` (the same flat shape as the existing `work-items/bugs/` registry, with `status: active|closed` frontmatter + `## Goal` / `## Children` slug list); each child work-item declares a single bare `Epic: <slug>` line in its `status.md`. Epic progress is DERIVED live from the children — a child counts as done by the SAME predicate `check-work-items-archival-stop.py` uses, resolving each slug across `work-items/active/` + `work-items/archive/` (the slug is stable across the close-move) — and is shown by `/agents-status` and `/agents-resume`. `$product-manager` admits the epic (the existing Coherence gate is the epic admission test); `$lead` links the children, keeps the roll-up, and closes the epic (`status: closed` + `## Closure`) only when ALL children are closed AND the goal is met. Governance-only — no new code, tooling, command, or hook; the epic DATA stays under gitignored `work-items/`, only the rules are committed. Shipped to BOTH packs symmetrically: Recovery rule (`CLAUDE.md` + `AGENTS.codex.md`), `$product-manager` + `$lead` role rules, the `status.md` format in `subagent-contracts.md`, the operating-model "where to save" persistence surface, and the status/resume/implement commands, plus a `docs/epics.md` reference + README pointer. Designed and deep-multiscope-opus-reviewed (24 confirmed findings applied: flat single-file structure corrected from a wrong "like bugs/performance folders" premise; `status:` key aligned with the bug registry; child resolution across active+archive; explicit edge cases; honest closure residual). **Known limitation (superseded by Batch B below):** at first ship epic closure was governance-only — the work-items-archival Stop hook did not scan `work-items/epics/`. The Batch B entry below closes this: the hook now flags ready-to-close and stale-closed epics.

- **Decision registry + cross-work-item dependencies — two enterprise task-memory gaps closed (Batch A.1).** Both follow the just-shipped Epic pattern (flat typed file or `status.md` join-key + derived views + role rules, both packs, governance-only). **(1) Decision registry:** a durable architecture decision that lived only inside a work-item's `design.md` was ARCHIVED WITH THE ITEM — a closed epic's key decisions got buried in `work-items/archive/` and were not queryable cross-cutting. Now `$architect` files cross-cutting/long-lived decisions in a flat `work-items/decisions/<date>-<slug>.md` registry (bug-style list-item frontmatter: `- id:`, `- status: proposed|accepted|dropped|superseded|reverted`, `- decided-by:`, `- context:`, `- supersedes:`, `- superseded-by:`) and a work-item's `design.md` REFERENCES the decision by id instead of duplicating it. Promotion `proposed -> accepted` is the `$architecture-reviewer` gate's call (not the author, not the archivist); semantic status transitions are owned by the deciding role (`$architect`/`$lead`), the `$knowledge-archivist` does ONLY non-semantic bookkeeping (the same lifecycle/bookkeeping split as Epics). Supersede is a stored TWO-WAY edge (`supersedes:` + `superseded-by:`). The decision status enum is INDEPENDENT of the work-item done-predicate — decisions are never "closed" by it. `/agents-status` lists `proposed` decisions + an accepted count. **(2) Cross-work-item dependencies:** there was no standing dependency edge — only the TRANSIENT `BLOCKED:prerequisite` gate verdict and unstructured PM dependency notes, so "what is blocked by what / what is ready to start" was invisible until a run was mid-flight. Now an optional multi-valued `Depends-on: <slug>, <slug>` line in `status.md` (next to the `Epic:` join-key) records a standing, planned inter-work-item dependency edge (related to but NOT identical to the runtime `BLOCKED:prerequisite` verdict, which files newly-discovered adjacent work in the bug registry — the `Depends-on` edge is a declared work-item→work-item dependency instead). Targets are WORK-ITEMS ONLY (resolved across `active/` + `archive/`; a slug matching a bug/epic/decision is dangling by design). `/agents-status` + `/agents-resume` derive `blocked-by` (open targets, same done-predicate as the archival hook), the `ready-set` (items whose every target is done), and flag dangling edges; `/agents-resume` warns before resuming a blocked item. Cycle-freedom is a `$lead` authoring rule (no live cycle detection in the MVP — that graph complexity was deliberately deferred). Shipped to BOTH packs symmetrically: `subagent-contracts` (`Depends-on` field), operating-model (Decision "where to save" row / Codex prose bullet), Recovery rule (`CLAUDE.md` + `AGENTS.codex.md`), `$lead` (`## Dependencies` + `## Decisions`), `$architect` (decision-authoring rule), `$product-manager` (record `Depends-on` from dependency notes), `$planner` (non-goal: cross-item deps are `$lead`'s, not per-item phases), the `/agents-status` + `/agents-resume` commands (Claude), plus `docs/decisions.md` + `docs/dependencies.md` references, a README pointer, and the `docs/README.md` curated index (which also retro-registers `docs/epics.md`). Designed and deep-multiscope-opus-reviewed (23 confirmed findings applied: bug-style frontmatter corrected from a wrong fenced-YAML premise; the `superseded-by:` back-link, the `dropped` terminal status, and the architecture-review acceptance authority added; archivist scoped to non-semantic bookkeeping only; `Depends-on` relabelled as persisted `BLOCKED:prerequisite` and scoped to work-items-only; live cycle detection dropped to an authoring rule; the missing `CLAUDE.md` Recovery surface and the PM rule added). **Known limitation:** like Epics, both are governance-enforced only — no hook scans `work-items/decisions/` or enforces the `Depends-on` edges, so a stale `proposed` decision or a forgotten blocker is not structurally caught; `/agents-status` surfacing the proposed-list and the blocked/ready/dangling views is the only standing prompt.

- **Lessons-loop registry + retrospective-on-closure + delivery-hygiene quick-wins (Batch A.2 — additive).** Closes three more enterprise task-memory gaps, same governance-only pattern as Epics + A.1 (flat typed registry / role rules / both packs / honest residual; data under gitignored `work-items/`, only the rules committed; ZERO spine additions — the spine is at its char cap). **(1) Lessons registry** `work-items/lessons/<date>-<slug>.md` (bug-style `- key:` frontmatter: `- id:`, `- status: open | applied | dropped | archived`, `- source:`, `- category: process|technical|governance|tooling`; body `## Lesson` / `## Context` / `## How to apply`): captures a delivery lesson IN-repo so it survives the work-item's archival, instead of vanishing or living only in the operator's out-of-repo personal memory. The ownership split mirrors the A.1 decisions registry EXACTLY — semantic status transitions are owned by the closing role that captured the lesson (`$lead` for lead-chains, main-conv for `requiresLead:false`), and `$knowledge-archivist` does ONLY non-semantic bookkeeping (it "does NOT decide a lesson status transition"). A stale-open accountability clause makes `$lead`/main-conv responsible for driving an `open` lesson to `applied` or `dropped` ("listing it is visibility, not closure"). The lessons-vs-personal-memory boundary is explicit: in-repo project task memory is canonical; promotion to the spine or personal memory is an ADDITIVE, one-directional, separate manual act. **(2) Retrospective-on-closure**: `closure.md` (and the epic `## Closure`) MUST carry a `Closed: <YYYY-MM-DD>` line and MAY include a `## Retrospective` (`What went well` / `What didn't` / `Lessons` filed in the registry by id), EXPECTED for substantial/troubled items and OPTIONAL for trivial ones (anti-ceremony — the close step stays mandatory, the retro within it is proportionate). **(3) Quick-wins**: a `Priority: high | medium | low` field on `status.md` (scheduling urgency, rendered as `prio:high` on the dashboard so it never collides with the bug/perf SEVERITY `[high]` brackets); acceptance-criteria IDs (`AC1/AC2/...` per phase, append-only/never-renumber for QA traceability); a `## Backlog` index section (admitted-but-not-started holding area); a milestone rule (epic-level, no new field); and `docs/definition-of-ready-done.md` — a vocabulary POINTER (not a duplicate checklist) mapping Definition-of-Ready/Done onto the existing PM admission + artifact gates and the Completion-reconciliation + QA + closure gates. Shipped to BOTH packs symmetrically (Claude role/contract/command files; Codex SKILL prose + lead-scan derivation where Claude uses `/agents-status`). **What A.2 does NOT do (honest residual):** it automates NOTHING — capture, status transitions, stale-lesson cleanup, duplicate-id validation, and retro-on-troubled-close are all human governance acts, prompted only when the operator runs `/agents-status` (or, on Codex, when the lead scans). No hook scans `work-items/lessons/` or verifies a troubled close got a retrospective. Designed + deep-multiscope-opus-reviewed (5 scopes — 2 BLOCKER + 8 MAJOR + 7 MINOR applied to a v3 spec) and a 3-angle implementation review-loop (Sonnet PASS / Codex REVISE / Consultant SHIP-AS-IS; 6 wording/parity fixes applied: epic-closure `Closed:` line, `docs/epics.md` closure format, lessons-enum pipe-form normalization, lifecycle-arrow precision, DoR/DoD pointer section names).

- **Bug registry: terminal states + format reconciliation (Batch A.2c).** The bug registry had a FORMAT SPLIT — `qa-engineer.md` PRINTED a YAML-`---`-fenced template (`title`/`found-in-phase`/`affected-surface`/...), but every on-disk `work-items/bugs/*.md` file and the project's own ADR/epic docs treated the `- key:` BULLET style as canonical. This change makes the bullet style the single canonical format: `qa-engineer.md` + the Codex `qa-engineer/SKILL.md` now print `- id:` / `- context:` / `- status:` / `- severity:` / `- area:` / `- found-by:` (the standalone `title` folds into the `# Bug:` H1; `found-in-phase` + `affected-surface` fold into `area`/`context`; the body goes free-form). The bug `status:` enum is extended `open | fixed` -> `open | fixed | wontfix | duplicate` (`wontfix` keeps a one-line reason; `duplicate` names the surviving bug id); a `## Bug status lifecycle` block on the Codex side gives the transition rule a home (Codex has no `/commands`), and Claude `agents-bugfix.md` cites `qa-engineer.md` as the canonical enum source so the enum is defined once per pack. Residual (honest): governance-enforced only — no hook validates that a `duplicate` names a real id or a `wontfix` carries its reason. Scope is the two format-owner files only; the ~12 engineer-role "Adjacent findings" references point at the format by pointer and are intentionally untouched (fixing the owner propagates for free). NOTE: the WEAK-MODEL example-only `src.gemini/` + `src.qwen/` packs still carry the old fenced format — out of scope here, to be picked up by the deferred provider-branch sync.

- **Structural backstops for epics + dependency/aging visibility + a ledger rollup (Batch B — code).** Four additive tooling changes. **(1) Epic-close Stop hook:** the work-items-archival hook (`check-work-items-archival-stop.py`, byte-identical in both packs) now ALSO scans `work-items/epics/` and blocks turn-end on a ready-to-close epic (every child done but the epic still `status: active`) or a stale-closed epic (`status: closed` but a child is not done) — closing the `docs/epics.md` "tracked follow-up". It reads the epic status from the `---` frontmatter ONLY and requires the documented `(active|closed)` child marker, so a prose bullet or an h3 sub-heading under `## Children` cannot false-block (two independent reviewers reproduced and the fix closed both false-positive vectors). Subagent-safe (skips on `agent_id`), fails open when `work-items/epics/` is absent, no installer change. **(2) Aging report + (3) blocker-state** in `scripts/check-work-items-state.py`: `--max-age-days <N>` flags active items whose `<date>-` directory prefix is older than N days, and the checker derives open `Depends-on` blockers + dangling targets from each item's `status.md`. Both are INFORMATIONAL (`info:` lines) and NEVER flip the exit code or the RESULT line — a blocked or aging active item is expected state, not a defect (operational-contract polarity). **(4) Ledger rollup:** `scripts/agent-run-ledger.py rollup` aggregates `agent-runs.jsonl` events (runs by role/execution-role/gate/status, evidence coverage, and a surfaced malformed-line count) for one work-item (`--work-item`) or all active items (`--root`); the top-level `--work-item` became optional (init/append guard on it) so `rollup --root` is non-breaking for the existing `--work-item <path> init/append` CLI. Covered by 22 new tests (epic-orphan cases incl. the FP regressions, aging/blocker info-only, rollup single/all-active/json/malformed, a done-predicate twin-drift guard); full suite 212 passed. Reviewed by a 3-angle code loop (Sonnet code-reviewer + silent-failure-hunter + Codex xhigh): the blocking hook's fail-open contract verified on every new path; the two MAJOR false-positive vectors fixed; three MINOR silent-under-report nits triaged (the rollup malformed-line surface adopted).

- **Reproducible provider-branch extractor (`scripts/extract-provider-branch.py`).** The 4 published standalone provider branches (claude/codex/gemini/qwen) drifted 100-207 commits behind main because the single-provider extraction was MANUAL. This adds a reusable, reviewed script that regenerates a provider's standalone tree from the monorepo deterministically: it includes that provider's `src.<provider>/` + `references-<provider>/` + `shared/` + `scripts/` + an allowlist of self-contained pack docs (epics, decisions, dependencies, lessons, definition-of-ready-done, work-item-execution-tracking); for Claude it GENERATES `src.claude/skills/agents-X/SKILL.md` from each `src.claude/commands/agents-X.md` (a deterministic, YAML-safe frontmatter wrapper); and it CARRIES FORWARD the standalone-adapted `README.md`/`INSTALL.md` and the branch's link-consistent curated docs (its `docs/README` index, agents-mode-reference, provider-runtime-layouts) rather than pulling the monorepo copies that link to excluded paths. Empirically validated NON-DESTRUCTIVE: extracting all 4 providers reproduces every existing branch file (0 dropped) and only adds the roadmap docs + staleness catch-up. Read-only against git, writes only under `--out` (refuses a non-empty dir or a git working tree), fails closed on a missing standalone file or an unresolved `--branch-ref`. Reviewed by a 2-angle loop (Sonnet code-reviewer + Codex xhigh): a real BLOCKER (an embedded `: ` in the agents-review-loop description produced invalid YAML) and the docs-curation broken-link gap were both fixed and covered by `tests/test_extract_provider_branch.py` (13 cases). The branches are not yet regenerated -- that is a separate, human-gated forward-commit step.

- **Cross-pack common-skill body-parity test (`tests/test_common_skill_parity.py`).** Closes a tracked validator gap: the shared common-skills (bug-hunting, analyzing-video-bugs, explain-simply, mathtype-book-page, windows-gui-manual-testing) are shipped per-pack, but NO pack validator content-checked them (they assert existence only and hash the shared *reference* files, not the skills), so a silent methodology drift in one pack would go uncaught. The new test asserts the skill BODY (everything after the YAML frontmatter) is byte-identical across all 4 packs. Investigating an initial full-file mismatch showed the only cross-pack difference is the frontmatter `description:` — deliberately provider-tailored (Codex ships terser descriptions; windows-gui names the provider) while every BODY is identical — so the test enforces body-parity (the real invariant: the methodology cannot drift) and allows the per-pack description, rather than forcing a spurious description rewrite. This was the only genuinely-open item of three tracked follow-ups carried from the 2026-06-02 audit; the other two are already resolved (the publication leak-scanner's MSYS-path-conversion coupling was fixed by the Python in-process matcher and its e2e test skips cleanly when bash/git are absent; the UNC + U+2026-ellipsis allowlist gaps were closed, leaving only the device-namespace UNC form and the forward-slash `//host/Users/` form as a documented, deliberate accepted-residual under-match to avoid device-namespace/URL false positives).

- **Runtime-first discipline now binds optimization/cleanup of working code, not only bug-fixes.** Folds in an external "confirm by runtime before changing working behavior" rule. The pre-fix diagnostic gate (always-on spine + the Bootstrap (a) trigger in both packs + the shared verification reference) now fires on a SECOND trigger: before the first code-mutating call that changes a path which already works, for speed/cleanup/refactor with NO reported defect — there runtime diagnosis or profiling before the edit is mandatory, and a new banned anti-pattern is "changing a working path on a code-hypothesis with no runtime baseline." `$performance-engineer` (both packs) sharpened: "Profile or diagnose the real bottleneck before optimizing — never on a code-hypothesis; distinguish computation from waiting (an idle timer, lock, or missed-signal wait reads as idle CPU in a profiler — a different defect class than slow computation)." Improved formulations, not new bulk: the spine pre-fix line was rewritten net −5 chars (dual-trigger replaces the verbose bug-only enumeration) while preserving all 88 manifest tokens. Spine 39893/39900, both pack validators green.

## 2026-06-10

### Added

- **Closed the work-item "create-but-never-close" asymmetry in the Recovery rule.** The `requiresLead: false` Recovery rule mandated that the main conversation *create* a work-item under `work-items/active/`, but the matching *close* step lived only in the `$lead` role (`closure.md` before `work-items/archive/`), which never runs for main-conversation-driven chains. Delivered items therefore piled up in `active/` forever (and the `/agents-implement` Completion step had no close step either). Fix: the Recovery rule in `src.claude/CLAUDE.md` and `src.codex/AGENTS.codex.md` now carries a symmetric, main-conversation-owned close step — when an item is delivered (committed/pushed) or parked, write `closure.md`, move the folder to `work-items/archive/<YYYY-MM>/<date>-<slug>/`, and move its `index.md` row to Archived; the close step is stated to be as mandatory as the create step. The `/agents-implement` Completion step now reinforces it. Why it matters: work-item task memory stays a truthful active/archived ledger instead of an ever-growing graveyard, so recovery and `/agents-status` reflect what is actually in flight.

- **Added a fifth structural hook: the work-items-archival Stop guard (`check-work-items-archival-stop.py`).** It is the structural backstop for the new close step — a warn-only text rule is exactly what was being ignored. At turn end it blocks the stop when an active item is unambiguously closed-but-not-moved: it has a `closure.md`, or its `status.md` has a state/status/stage/outcome line whose value begins with a done/closed word (`closed`/`done`/`complete`/`archived`). Detection is anchored to the state-key line, not free prose anywhere in the file, so chatty active-item text (`nothing pending on our side`, `phase 1 shipped + pushed`) never produces a false block — a multi-angle review caught that the original free-substring prose tier false-positived on normal active items, and it was replaced with this state-line-anchored detector. A merely-active or parked item never triggers. The block payload names each orphan and the close procedure; the per-stop `[acknowledge-open-work-items]` marker overrides one decision. **Subagent-safe by construction:** it is registered only on the `Stop` event (never `SubagentStop`) and skips immediately when the envelope carries `agent_id`, so it can never block a subagent — work-item lifecycle is the main conversation's job. Shipped byte-identical to both packs (`agents/scripts/` Claude, `skills/lead/scripts/` Codex), auto-installed by all four installers as a second `Stop` entry, with structural-enforcement docs updated (four → five hooks, three blocking + two audit) across `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.codex.md`, and `INSTALL.md`. Covered by `tests/test_work_items_archival_hook.py` (20 cases × both packs, including the subagent-not-blocked invariant and active-item false-positive guards for `nothing pending` / phase-level `shipped + pushed` / `closed-loop`).

### Fixed

- **Retrofitted the same `agent_id` subagent-safety skip onto the passive-polling Stop hook.** Both blocking Stop guards now exit immediately on a subagent envelope, so neither can interfere with a subagent doing its work. Applied byte-identical to both pack copies; main-conversation behavior is unchanged (the early return fires only when `agent_id` is present).

- **The bugfix-discipline PreToolUse guard no longer false-blocks doc/report/scratch/plan/task-memory writes.** A write whose target path is under `.reports/`, `.scratch/`, `.plans/`, `work-items/`, or `docs/` (matched as a `/`-bounded path segment) now exits 0 — those are never the CODE fix the guard exists to catch. Root cause was proven on a real failing transcript: the guard fired *legitimately* on a `.reports/` memo write because the surrounding prompt was a bug-fix-plan review dense with bug vocabulary and the headless run did not emit the `[skip-bugfix-discipline]` prose marker — not the "hook re-read its own deny text" mechanism an advisory had guessed (that hypothesis was disproven against the transcript: `extract_user_typed_text` already skips `tool_result`). Fix applied byte-identical to both packs; `apply_patch` (paths in the patch body) stays fully guarded; code writes/edits in a bug context still deny (no hole). Verified repro-then-gone plus no-hole across 11 probes; regression tests in `tests/test_bugfix_discipline_hook.py::TestBugfixExemptPaths` (10 cases × both packs).

## 2026-06-09

### Added

- **Added the "explain plainly to humans" rule, folded into the always-on spine.** Human-facing output (chat status, explanations, decisions) must lead with plain meaning and a clear what-to-do — numbered choices when a decision is needed — not a bare dump of identifiers or jargon; brevity modes (e.g. caveman) drop filler but never license a jargon dump. The full rule is `shared/references/spine/explain-plainly-to-humans.md`; its essence is folded into the always-on `Documentation terminology discipline` card (the spine was at its hard char cap, so some terminology refinements moved from the always-on card to the section extract while the four core terminology teeth stayed always-on; net −11 chars). Why it matters: it makes the operator-facing communication standard — lead with meaning and the decision, keep dense identifiers in commits/code — a first-class always-loaded rule, and it explicitly overrides brevity modes so terseness never becomes a jargon dump. Verified by an independent deep governance review (PASS, no blockers) plus the spine and all four pack validators.

### Fixed

- **The bugfix-discipline PreToolUse guard no longer blocks subagent tool calls.** A subagent's hook envelope carries `agent_id` and runs with `transcript_path` pointing at the MAIN session transcript (confirmed by captured envelopes — a subagent fire has `agent_id`, a main-conversation fire does not). The guard therefore keyed on the main conversation's last genuine user message — a possibly stale or unrelated bug-trigger — for a subagent's edit, and the subagent cannot inject the `[skip-bugfix-discipline]` override into that main transcript (it writes to its own isolated context). The result was an un-overridable false positive that denied every subagent (and Workflow) edit for the rest of any session whose recent main-conversation message tripped a trigger. Fix: the guard skips when `agent_id` is present — the dispatching main conversation owns the diagnostic discipline at the dispatch decision, and the subagent runs a vetted scoped task, not the human's raw bug report. Applied to both pack copies (`src.claude` + `src.codex`); main-conversation gating is byte-unchanged (the early-return fires only for `agent_id`-present envelopes). Verified with crafted envelopes against one trigger transcript: subagent+trigger → allow, main+trigger+no-discipline → deny, main+no-trigger → allow.

## 2026-06-05

### Added

- **Added `$explain-simply` as a provider-neutral common skill and registered it across Codex, Claude, Gemini, and Qwen.** The skill gives agents a reusable lane for clear explanatory answers: define terms on first use, preserve uncertainty, prefer simple examples, and avoid inventing facts. It is shipped from the source packs so global installs receive the same behavior instead of depending on stale local `.agents` copies.

- **Added two compact shared-governance rules with spine references: `General-case over local symptoms` and `Reuse before hand-rolling`.** The first prevents narrow patches when the verified cause belongs to a broader owner, invariant, lifecycle, or pipeline; the second requires checking repo-standard mechanisms, installed dependencies, framework features, and mature optimized library/tool options before custom-building generic capabilities. Validators now pin the new rules so future spine compression cannot silently drop their operational teeth.

### Changed

- **Strengthened the new general-case and reuse rules around correct abstraction and stack selection.** The shared spine now makes explicit that agents must find the correct concept/abstraction level, prefer correctness over development speed unless the user explicitly scopes prototype/fastest/throwaway work, and treat stack choice as a correctness, fit, maintainability, and performance decision rather than a development speed/convenience shortcut. The detailed spine references add the operational ladder from visible symptom to common concept, owner, invariant, sibling cases, and smallest correct implementation.

- **Disambiguated speed in the new abstraction and stack-choice rules.** `Speed` in the shortcut warning now means development/delivery speed, not application runtime speed. Runtime speed, latency, throughput, and responsiveness remain performance constraints, and for application optimization work runtime speed may be the primary stack-choice constraint.

- **Shortened long Codex utility-skill descriptions to reduce skill-budget truncation warnings.** The affected skill bodies and workflows are unchanged; only the frontmatter descriptions for discovery/routing were compressed so Codex keeps more room for the full installed skill inventory.

- **Extended pack validation for common-skill parity and install readiness.** Claude, Gemini, and Qwen validators now require every Common skills entry to have a shipped `SKILL.md`; Codex keeps its description-budget guard while allowing the new skill inventory. This matters because a common skill must be present in the source packs before global install, not only in an already-installed local runtime.

## 2026-06-03

### Added

- **Integrated the autonomous parallel-review-loop methodology into the monorepo as an installed, contract-backed surface (completes the follow-up named in the universal-premise entry below).** The loop runs three independent scope angles — two SMART verdict angles plus one mechanical SCOUT that casts no verdict — against ONE written fix-design artifact, revises and re-dispatches autonomously under a per-round anti-drift guard, caps at N=3 rounds, and gates the human only at convergence (or on drift/deadlock). It is distinct from the adjacent surfaces: not a single advisory opinion (`/agents-second-opinion`), not disjoint parallel helper lanes (`/agents-external-brigade`), and not a post-implementation specialist chain (`/agents-review`).
  - **Installed-contract design (not prose triplication).** The provider-neutral design trunk `shared/references/review-loop-methodology.md` stays the canonical methodology but is NOT installed into any runtime; each pack ships a thin RUNTIME binding that carries the operative rules and the pack-specific dispatch. Claude gets `src.claude/agents/contracts/review-loop.md` (the installed contract `/agents-review-loop` reads at runtime, because `shared/references/` is not installed) plus the thin command `src.claude/commands/agents-review-loop.md`; Codex gets `src.codex/skills/review-loop/` (SKILL.md + `agents/openai.yaml`). The command and skill READ their installed contract rather than restating the methodology, so the prose has one owner per layer.
  - **Claude dispatch mapping with an explicit anti-consolidation rule.** Surgical verdict → `external-reviewer` (routes to the external provider, Codex); deep verdict → `architecture-reviewer` at `model: opus`; mechanical scout → `analyst` at `model: sonnet` (factual `file:line`, no verdict — it feeds the verdicts, and is intentionally `analyst`, never `qa-engineer`). The deep angle is a direct-verdict reviewer and is intentionally NOT `subagent_type: consultant` — consultant's external mode shells out to its own provider (the documented role-confusion that returns "standing by" instead of a verdict); `consultant.md` is left untouched. The Codex binding mirrors the scope model but notes it is NOT the Claude background-Agent loop — Codex runs the angles through the external-brigade / sequential-synthesis surface, concurrently when supported, else sequentially.
  - **B-refined autonomy.** The loop converges WITHOUT a human gate per round — the human sees only the converged result or an honest stop. Anti-drift is the per-round course-keeper that replaces the human while the loop runs: every round it verifies the revised artifact still serves the original pinned objective (goal, admitted scope, runtime-verified root all unchanged), and a goal-shift / scope-widening / new-unverified-premise is drift that stops the loop. Re-dispatching an unchanged artifact is forbidden (a verdict cannot change on identical input), and the runtime-verified root is a hard gate — a second-hand root (commit message, prior plan, report) is runtime-verified this session first, and is never pinned `CONFIRMED — do not re-litigate`.
  - **Ledger + structural validator backstop.** A shipped loop must persist a per-round `review-loop-state` record under `.scratch/reviews/` (pinned objective/scope/runtime_root identical across rounds, per-round diff, both verdict angles + the scout, no bare PASS, round ≤ cap). The new `scripts/validate-review-loop-state.{py,sh,ps1}` validates that ledger's STRUCTURE only — it deliberately does not and cannot check semantics (whether the reasoning was sound stays review territory) — and ships a `--self-test` that accepts a sample-good and rejects a sample-bad ledger. The validator is a development/CI tool kept in `scripts/` and is deliberately NOT installed into the global runtime (the global stays clean of dev-only tooling); the runtime backstop is the persisted ledger trace itself. The four installers now also seed `/.scratch/` into the project-level local-only `.gitignore` (alongside the existing `/.reports/` and `/work-items/`) so the artifacts and ledgers stay local-only. The Codex `review-loop` skill is registered in `validate-skill-pack.sh` (utility-skill list + core-file/`openai.yaml` checks; the description-budget total cap was bumped 3700→3800 as a visible decision to fit the new 65-char description at ~3756). Why it matters: the discipline that gets independent multi-angle convergence on a fix BEFORE it lands is now a first-class repo surface with a durable installed contract and an auditable per-round trace, instead of an ad hoc personal skill — and the structural validator gives the autonomous loop a mechanical backstop without overclaiming that structure proves soundness.

### Changed

- **Consolidated the governance spine's verify-family into one core anchor and reordered its 8 sections by importance (2-round review-loop-gated).** `shared/AGENTS.shared.md` gained a single `Verification-and-evidence core` anchor; the `Ambiguity resolution discipline`'s redundant 13-item evidence-sources list (a duplicate of the `Evidence-citation discipline`'s four categories) collapsed into a named reference — no enforceable rule dropped (63/63 manifest tokens, 21/21 parity; the Hypothesis-disclosure, Evidence-citation, Active-availability cards and the RULE №0 second-hand-root clause left byte-unchanged), net −38 chars (headroom 3→41). Separately the 8 top-level sections were reordered by ascending importance so the strongest-retention slot (the document tail) holds the heaviest enforcement (Core delegation → Engineering hygiene → Publication safety), with reference/lookup material front-loaded — the principle that an always-loaded file's end is adhered to most strongly. A 2-round autonomous review loop (Codex surgical + architecture-reviewer@opus verdicts, analyst@sonnet scout) converged only after round 1 caught a fat-anchor design silently dropping the `likely`/`in general` correctness-drivers; the landed lean design avoids that by leaving the donor cards intact. Deferred non-blocking follow-ups: pin `likely` in the validator manifest; re-express the size guard band in bytes if Claude's 40k warning proves byte-based.

- **Kept the review-loop ledger validator repo/dev-only — not installed to the global.** Per the directive that the global install carries no repo-specific dev tooling, `validate-review-loop-state.{py,sh,ps1}` (the ledger-schema CI check, with a `--self-test`) was removed from the four installers' runtime-helper set and from the already-installed `~/.claude` and `~/.codex`; it stays in `scripts/` for development/CI. The loop's runtime backstop is the persisted per-round ledger trace itself; the schema check stays in the repo. The installed global pack now carries only what the runtime needs — the methodology contract/command/skill — not validation tooling.

- **Corrected the new `review-loop` skill's stated description length (67→65 chars) in this release note and the validator budget comment, and tightened the design trunk's binding scope to "each production pack".** Surfaced by the review-loop's own final dogfood gate (the recursive self-review of the shipped integration): the skill's single-line description measures 65 chars, not 67, and only the two production packs (Claude + Codex) ship a runtime binding. Documentation-only — no behavior change; the live description total (3756, reported authoritatively by the validator's pass line) and the 3800 cap are unchanged.

- **Made the verify-before-acting / no-unverified-hypothesis discipline UNIVERSAL — it no longer reads as bug-fix-only.** The user identified that the rule had landed mostly in bug-fix / implementation contexts (the Bootstrap "before every fix", Ambiguity-resolution's "for a bug or runtime failure", the Pre-fix diagnostic gate), so building a **plan, review loop, or any work on an unverified second-hand root** — a commit-message diagnosis, a prior plan, an agent report — fell outside every bug-scoped trigger and was never challenged. Concrete incident that motivated it: five review loops plus plans v1→v4 plus two failed fixes were all built on a commit-message diagnosis that a five-minute runtime measurement later showed was reversed, with the root even pinned "CONFIRMED — do not re-litigate" so the loops never re-examined it. The fix adds **no new fifth rule and no dangling rule-name** — it strengthens the existing universal owners:
  - **Spine Core-delegation (`shared/AGENTS.shared.md`)** now treats a second-hand root or diagnosis (commit message, prior plan/report, subagent output, `PASS`/completion claim) as a hypothesis until verified by the orchestrating session or next gate, and forbids pinning it `CONFIRMED` or building plans, review loops, or fixes on it. This folds in (and replaces) the older narrower "do not trust subagent reports/`PASS` without verification" clause.
  - **Spine Ambiguity-resolution** was de-scoped from "for a bug or runtime failure" to "any decision, plan, review verdict, root-cause claim, fix, or behavior change [that] rests on a premise" — the same evidence-capture-and-rule-out-alternatives procedure now binds all work, not only bug reports.
  - **Bootstrap (both packs, `src.claude/CLAUDE.md` + `src.codex/AGENTS.codex.md`)** was reframed so its heading and STOP lead with the universal premise rule ("every decision, plan, review verdict, fix, implementation action, or behavior-changing commit must rest on verified premises") and present the two trigger moments as additional **edit/commit checkpoints that do not limit the universal rule**; step 1 generalized from "drive this fix" to "drive this decision or change".
  - **Reference parity:** the matching extracts in `shared/references/spine/delegation-principles.md` and `shared/references/spine/verification-and-decision-discipline.md` were updated to the universal wording. The conceptual companion `evidence-based-answer-pipeline.md` was deliberately left untouched — its governance list already references the disciplines by name and editing it would duplicate the rule the spine now owns.
  - Cap-safe: the spine stays **39,897 / 39,900** chars (net +3, no compaction needed — the rewrite reused the space freed by folding the old subagent-trust clause into the new second-hand-root teeth) with all 63 protection-manifest tokens and the pinned bug-trigger phrases intact (the bug-trigger tokens dropped from Ambiguity-resolution survive on the Pre-fix gate). The design went through a three-angle review loop (Codex `gpt-5.5`/xhigh + Sonnet pr-review; the consultant angle was orchestrator-covered after the consultant subagent's recurring role-confusion), and the Codex-vs-Sonnet disagreement on the char cost (+3 vs ~+230) was resolved by running the validator rather than trusting either estimate. Why it matters: the discipline that stops a tower of work being built on an unverified foundation now binds the **foundation moment** (planning, research, review loops), not only the edit/commit moment where the bug-scoped hooks already fire. Known follow-ups (each its own review-looped pass): consolidate the remaining verify-family overlaps (`:24`/`:81`/`:91` all repeat "don't guess; verify; rule out alternatives") to free sustainable spine room; give the consultant review-angle a distinct model/role/visibility so it stops colliding with the other reviewers; integrate the `parallel-review-loops` skill into the repo; and a priority-ordered spine re-sort.

- **Backported ~13 new generic `mathtype-book-page` methodology blocks from active downstream Codex use, caught by the pre-install divergence gate.** Before reinstalling, the standing mathtype divergence check compared the repo's generalized skill against the live `~/.codex/skills/mathtype-book-page/SKILL.md` and found new generic methodology iterated downstream during real book-translation work, mixed with book-specific instantiation. The generic bullets were merged into all four packs byte-identical (1101→1157 lines, additive plus two small in-place list refinements): adjacent-figure re-inspection after a repair, rendered-figure-text / OCR-residue gates, bitmap-crop insertion with a `pdfimages`-style image-object probe, bitmap-label localization, short OCR-fragment detection near formulas, full-range OCR/provenance-leak removal, source negation/modality preservation (`not`/`only`/`except`/`unless`), reference/index heading translation, references-tail duplicate-heading repair, formula-adjacent connector translation, merged multi-number display splitting, an inline indexed-variable-list group check, a dense-matrix MathType TeX-donor fallback, multi-number row-split table traps, body-font / source-table / font-size render-grounded checks, prose-list inline-MathType punctuation, multi-line number-cell layout, no-break inline punctuation, lost superscript/star OCR detection, prose/OLE residue rejection, and a guard that the agent `Terms and Abbreviations` glossary does not leak into the translated book deliverable. Two downstream bullets carrying real author names and a publisher were generalized to `<Surname>` / `<Author>` / `<Publisher>` placeholders. The book-specific instantiation in the downstream copy — the chapter→code map, chunk numbers, audit-wave dates, specific output values, manifest enums, release paths — was deliberately NOT backported per the skill's Repo-Independence Contract, verified by a zero-hit concrete-leak scan over the added lines. The reconcile was delegated to a knowledge-archivist agent and independently re-verified (byte-identity, additive-only, leak-scan, all four pack validators PASS) before commit. Why it matters: the mathtype skill is a two-way channel the user iterates in real book-translation work, so the pre-install gate prevents a blind repo→global reinstall from silently discarding downstream methodology, and this backport captures the reusable methodology while keeping the shipped pack book-agnostic. The **Codex reinstall is intentionally held** — it would overwrite the user's live project-instantiated `~/.codex` copy with the generalized version (the book specifics live in the runbook, not the skill).

- **Added the `Indirect-regression discipline` rule so a change is no longer waved off as innocent just because it did not edit the broken component.** A recurring reasoning failure — "I didn't touch that component's code, so my change can't have broken it", compounded by "proving" innocence on a convenient proxy state (a bisect at scroll-0 when the symptom is at scroll-to-end) — now has a named always-on governance rule. The shared spine (`shared/AGENTS.shared.md`, after Regression hygiene) carries the compact binding form: a regression appearing after your change is NOT exonerated by "the edited code does not touch that component" — behavior couples indirectly through timing, lifecycle, render/layout, shared state, and viewport; your change is the prime suspect and you clear it ONLY by reverting it and reproducing the REAL symptom, not a proxy. The full rule (with the complete system-coupling list and the bisect-on-the-symptom procedure) lives in `shared/references/spine/verification-and-decision-discipline.md`, and a new Pre-fix Bootstrap violation trigger fires in both `src.claude/CLAUDE.md` and `src.codex/AGENTS.codex.md` before a fix edit. Because the spine was at its hard size cap, ~10 verbose/redundant rules were compacted to make room (validator-confirmed: all 63 protection-manifest tokens and every pack-pinned phrase preserved); a three-angle review loop (Codex + Sonnet pr-review) caught and reverted two over-compactions that had removed real requirements — a State-synchronization verification default and the Markdown "several short one-line" phrase the four pack validators pin — both restored cap-neutrally. Why it matters: the failure mode is a confident-but-wrong dismissal that ships regressions; the rule shifts the burden onto the change author to PROVE non-involvement by a symptom-faithful revert, not assert it from untouched code. It originated as a runtime-file hack (an edit straight into the installed `~/.claude/AGENTS.md`, present in neither the repo source nor the Codex runtime); doing it in the repo source means it now propagates to every pack on install instead of being silently overwritten. (Follow-up: the spine clause was then tightened — its opening matches the failure-mode voice ("I didn't edit that component"), and the dropped "name what your change altered" diagnostic step plus the "burden is to PROVE" emphasis were restored, net +8 chars, still within cap.)

- **Added a git-history-hygiene rule: clean self-introduced broke-it-then-fixed-it churn before the first push.** The Bootstrap "Recovery readiness" step (in `src.claude/CLAUDE.md` and `src.codex/AGENTS.codex.md`) already preferred `git reset --hard` over `git revert` for local hypothesis-failure commits; it now also binds the general case — a bug you introduced in one local commit and corrected in a later one should not survive into pushed history. While the commits are still local, squash or reset the churn so the published history shows the correct fix directly, not the intermediate self-made error and its correction; clean it before the first push rather than force-pushing already-published history after the fact. Why it matters: a clean history makes `git blame` and `git log` show intent rather than the author's stumbles, and stops wrong intermediate states being preserved as "partial truths" — the same reason the spine already prefers `reset --hard` over `revert` for a failed-hypothesis commit.

## 2026-05-30

### Added

- **Added a new-session orientation guide so maintainers do not have to rediscover the source/runtime boundary every session.** `docs/new-session-guide.md` is now the first-read guide for Orchestrarium maintenance sessions, linked from `README.md`, `docs/README.md`, `AGENTS.md`, and `CLAUDE.md`. It states the source-first rule directly: tracked Orchestrarium source is canon, while `~/.codex/`, `~/.claude/`, project `.agents/`, and project `.claude/` are installed runtime outputs to sync only after the owning source file is fixed. It also maps the main source owners, runtime surfaces, common maintenance patterns, validation commands, and "do not" cases such as patching Claude-specific Agent mechanics into Codex by analogy. Why it matters: recent incidents came from starting in stale installed surfaces or having to re-explain that this monorepo is the primary source; the guide makes that orientation durable for new sessions.

- **Conversational terminology discipline added to the shared governance spine.** The existing `Documentation terminology discipline` rule required a `## Terms and Abbreviations` section only in human-facing DOCS; the main conversation's live CHAT replies still emitted unexplained jargon, acronyms, and domain/role/provider/tool terms, which the user reported as unreadable ("в чате mainconv постоянно сыплет ими и ничего не поймешь что он говорит"). The rule in `shared/AGENTS.shared.md` now also binds term-dense conversational replies: expand jargon inline or list the terms. It was added as a compact clause to the spine's existing rule rather than as a new card or a cap bump — per the spine validator's own documented policy (`scripts/validate-agents-spine.py`), a genuinely new always-on rule is the trigger to keep the operational card compact, not to raise the size ceiling; the spine went 39,815 → 39,885 chars, still 15 under the 39,900 cap. Why it matters: governance previously made published docs readable to outside readers but left the live working conversation dense with insider shorthand, so the human steering the work could not follow it.

### Changed

- **Codified "read broad, change narrow" in the shared governance spine.** The minimization disciplines (`Change-surface minimization`, `smallest safe reversible change`) govern what a change *touches*, not what it *reads* — but that boundary was implicit and was being misapplied as license to read code narrowly (a few lines at a time) to conserve context, which under-reads and yields wrong changes. The `Readability and local reasoning` rule in `shared/AGENTS.shared.md` now states it explicitly: "Before changing, read what the change needs, not a narrow slice; minimization bounds the change, not the read." It was added as a compact clause with no cap bump (per the spine validator's own policy a genuinely new always-on rule keeps its operational card compact rather than raising the size ceiling); the adjacent `secondary checks` line was tightened to recover headroom, leaving the spine at 39,887 / 39,900. Why it matters: correctness depends on reading enough surrounding context — the whole function, its call sites and dependents — so reading too little to save tokens is a false economy, while the minimization rules correctly bound only the change surface, not the reading.

### Fixed

- **Made "stop closeout, work" a hard continuation trigger instead of an acknowledgement trap.** The existing continuity rules already said a passed slice is not goal completion and that `дальше` / `go` / `продолжай` continue the next incomplete item, but they did not explicitly cover user corrections such as "завязывай с closeout, работай". Agents could therefore answer with a bare acknowledgement like "OK, closeout removed" and still stop. Shared governance now treats `stop closeout`, `завязывай с closeout`, `работай`, and equivalent continue-working corrections as instructions to immediately take the next concrete action inside the active task unless genuinely blocked. The rule is mirrored into the lead operating surfaces, agents-mode continuity reference, shared subagent operating model, and new-session guide. Why it matters: the user should not have to spend every new session re-teaching Codex that a closeout correction means continue working, not produce another meta-summary.

- **Made context compaction a first-class continuity trigger instead of a license to summarize and stop.** The shared spine already said side requests do not replace the primary task and that partial batches are not completion, but it did not name the context-compaction/resume-from-summary transition explicitly. In practice that allowed an agent to wake up after a compacted summary, trust stale sliced evidence, emit a final-style status, or stop instead of restoring the active task. The shared `AGENTS.shared.md` primary-task rule now says context compaction may interrupt but not replace the task; on resume from a summary, the agent must restore the primary task, next unchecked step, and open evidence gates before acting. The same rule is mirrored into the lead operating surfaces and the `agents-mode` continuity references for Codex, Claude, Gemini, and Qwen. Why it matters: a compacted summary is a recovery artifact, not a closeout signal; required gates such as full-window crop validation must survive the context transition.

- **Removed the Qt-only bias from `$windows-gui-manual-testing` and made crop validation an early hard gate across the visual-video workflow.** The Codex UI metadata exposed the common skill as "Qt Windows GUI Manual Testing" and its default prompt asked for "a Qt desktop UI", while the skill description and shared common-skill index also led with Qt wording. That made the skill look toolkit-specific even though its workflow already covered native Windows, WebView/native-child, and other desktop surfaces. The Codex `agents/openai.yaml` now names it "Windows GUI Manual Testing", the provider skill descriptions and overview text name Windows desktop GUI broadly, and the shared index/README stop describing it as Qt-first. Separately, the crop rule that previously lived deep inside `$windows-gui-manual-testing` step 3 is now a top-level hard crop gate in both `$windows-gui-manual-testing` and `$analyzing-video-bugs`: before judging cropped screenshots, videos, or extracted frames as full-window evidence, the agent must verify the crop against an uncropped full-frame/full-desktop image and confirm the full target window is present, including the top-right close button for normal overlapped windows; clipped crops must be discarded and re-extracted. The live `~/.codex/skills/` copies were updated with the same wording so current Codex sessions see the corrected metadata before the next reinstall. Why it matters: the reported downstream-project failures were not caused by missing visual methodology — the methodology existed — but by trigger/UI wording and buried crop validation that let agents take a "convenient" crop before proving it was valid.

- **Closed the UNC machine-local-path leak gap and fixed the U+2026 ellipsis-placeholder false positive in the shared allowlist owner.** Two follow-ups against `check-machine-local-path.py` (both packs, kept byte-identical) — the single allowlist owner consumed by the `machine-local-path` PreToolUse hook directly and by the publication leak-scanner via in-process import of `find_machine_paths`, so each change applies to both surfaces at once. (1) **UNC host/share homes now block.** The owner previously detected only drive-letter and POSIX/MSYS homes, so a concrete UNC home such as `\\host\Users\<name>` or `\\server\share\...\Users\<name>` written into a tracked file slipped past both the hook warning and the scanner block — the gap the prior scanner entry recorded as a tracked follow-up. A new left-anchored `_PATTERNS` entry now flags genuine UNC user homes (host or host+share, including deep shares) while a deliberate left-anchor plus host-label requirement keeps it from self-matching a drive-prefixed escaped Windows path literal such as the JSON `"C:\\Users\\test"` (a doubled `\\Users\\` with no host label before it is not a UNC path); the `\\?\` and `\\.\` device namespaces are excluded by a negative lookahead because their embedded drive form is already caught by the drive-letter pattern. Accepted residual under-match (left out to avoid device-namespace / URL false positives): `\\?\UNC\host\share\...` and forward-slash `//host/Users/...`. (2) **U+2026 ellipsis placeholder no longer false-positives.** A documentation path segment written with the Unicode horizontal ellipsis U+2026 instead of an ASCII `...` run — e.g. `C:\Users\...` in backslash, forward-slash, or bare form, or a mixed dot+ellipsis segment — was treated as a concrete account name and flagged; the placeholder filter now accepts the U+2026 character (and any dot/ellipsis mix) as an ellipsis placeholder, matching the existing ASCII `...` behavior. The no-Python fallback regex in `check-publication-safety.sh` is intentionally NOT extended for UNC (it is a rare degraded path that already over-blocks). Covered by new gate-safe regression rows in both `tests/test_machine_local_path_hook.py` and `tests/test_publication_safety_scanner.py` (UNC and ellipsis fixtures are assembled from runtime fragments so the test sources never self-trip the scanner). Why it matters: the hook and the publication gate now catch a real machine-local-path leak class they previously missed (UNC homes), while stopping a false alarm on a legitimate documentation placeholder, so the gate stays both stricter where it should block and quieter where it should pass.

- **Publication leak-scanner now allowlists placeholder paths — and in doing so closed three real machine-local-path leak gaps it had.** `check-publication-safety.sh` (both packs) blocked `git push` whenever a staged file contained `C:\Users\<name>` / `C:\Users\...` — placeholder documentation (the machine-local-path hook's own examples in INSTALL.md / RELEASE_NOTES.md / AGENTS.codex.md) that is safe and already public — because its `[A-Za-z]:\\Users\\` pattern matched the full content of staged files with no allowlist. It recurred on every commit touching those docs and forced a separate security-reviewer clearance each time. The scanner now routes path-pattern candidate lines through the existing `check-machine-local-path.py` allowlist (`find_machine_paths`, resolved relative to each scanner's own location — single source of truth, no duplicated token list), so placeholders pass while concrete machine-local paths still block; non-path patterns (secrets, tokens, transcript markers) stay an unconditional block path; and if Python is unavailable it falls back to a refined regex plus a degraded-mode stderr notice, never fail-open (fail-safe = over-block). A security-engineer designed the change (approach B) and a security-reviewer gated it. In the process it closed three leak gaps the old scanner silently had: (1) forward-slash and leading-slash home forms (`C:/Users/`, `/home/`, `/Users/`, `/c/Users/`) were never matched on Windows because the bundled MSYS bash rewrites leading-`/` arguments before `git grep` sees them — the in-process Python matcher is immune; (2) workstation dev roots (`D:\dev\`, even the repository's own root) were not detected at all; (3) lowercase `c:\users\` slipped past the case-sensitive pattern.
